On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 3 v1.0

# The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field

1\. Purpose  
This paper is an Adjudication Form (an auxiliary form of the inversion-discernment group). It is used, for a declared host instance frozen in advance, to adjudicate from the declared observations whether the fact of constitution of a Sedimentation-Field holds on the instance, and to give an adjudication domain valid in both directions, the bounds of Misjudgment, and the reasons for ⊥.  
The properties adjudicated fall into two layers.  
The first layer consists of operationalized predicates: the rest retention difference Tr^rest, the cut-off retention difference Tr^cut, the write difference Wr, the historical retention difference Hist, the in-protocol active-participation witness Act\_𝓟, and the dependence effects Dep and Dep₀. They are defined on the state transitions and legitimate interventions of the declared model and are given in Section 3.  
The second layer consists of the three duties of *The General Form of Field Concretization* and their components: the Trace-Response-set duty TraceSet (components q, r), the organizational-participation duty Part, the historical-dynamics duty HDyn (components Act, HistCond), and the fact of constitution Fld(χ). By Theorem 1 and Corollary 1 of the General Form, within its working domain  
　　Fld(χ) ⇔ ∃T \[ TraceSet(χ, T) ∧ Part(χ, T) ∧ HDyn(χ, T) \],  
where Theorem 1 gives ⇒ and the definitional correspondence of Corollary 1 gives ⇐. The evidence atoms and read-back of the three duties are given by *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*.  
The bridging between the two layers and the limits of adjudication are the central problem of this Form. The duties are functions of mechanism witnesses, not of state trajectories (Theorem 1 of the Mathematical Form); the first-layer predicates depend only on state-level transitions and interventions, and so cannot adjudicate the duties in both directions (Theorem 5). Mechanism-level interventions—process-by-process knockout, local setting of Trace-Response positions, faithful read-out—reconstruct exactly, under the declared instance protocols, the action, source, formation and trace-retention atoms of the F layer of the Mathematical Form (Theorem 6). Hence, within the adjudication subdomain where the antecedents of each verdict are met item by item—the legitimate perturbations that can be applied have been covered, the inventory is complete, the encoding is faithful—the duties and Fld can be adjudicated in both directions on F-class hosts (Theorem 7); elsewhere the verdict is ⊥. Supplementing state-level observation with structural declarations yields several conditional positive determinations (Lemmas 10–12).  
The range of values of each verdict depends on the evidence level:  
　With state-level observation Y₁ only: q gives only 0; Part and HDyn give 0 only when “every position of T stays no greater than the resolution within W”; everything else gives ⊥.  
　Y₁ plus declarations Y\_decl: under an F-class declaration q can give 1; when the position inventory is complete (Dom5, state coverage, not including the process inventory), Fld has a degenerate negative determination; under structural declarations there are in addition conditional positive determinations of r, Part and HDyn.  
　Mechanism-level observation Y₂ (model mode): within the adjudication subdomain where the antecedents of each verdict are met item by item, the three duties and Fld are adjudicated in both directions; everything else gives ⊥. A verdict of 1 requires the listed protocols and one certified witness; a verdict of 0 further requires that a legitimate perturbation has been applied at every candidate position (otherwise ⊥\_Pert, ⊥\_Obs), that the inventory is complete and the records faithful (INV-c, REC, ENT, POS; otherwise ⊥\_Inv, ⊥\_Rec, ⊥\_Id), and that the encoding is faithful (EF1–EF5); Part retains ⊥\_Sub at processes invisible below the resolution. This is not a global adjudication of all F-class models. These are verdicts on F models; when reading back to a material instance, a verdict of 1 further requires IMP and Dom4, and a verdict of 0 further requires EF1–EF5 (Theorem 7).  
　Measurement mode: gives only 1 or ⊥.  
Preconditions of adjudication:  
(1) The definitions of the properties are closed. The first-layer predicates are defined by Section 3; the second-layer duties are defined by the master formula of Section 5 and the bearing relation of Section 3 of the General Form, and their evidence atoms by D16–D19 of the Mathematical Form.  
(2) The observation set is delimited: Section 3, 3.5.  
(3) The forward relation is given by established Forms: the forward relation from duties to observations is the F-layer recursion of 4.2 of the Mathematical Form, that is, the net increment equals the sum of the effective components and effective pulses; the reading on the instance is borne by the instance protocols of 4.2 in Section 4, and failure of a protocol enters the bounds of Misjudgment.  
Qualification boundary. A first-layer predicate with value 1 is evidence for the corresponding duty; for it to acquire qualification for that duty, it must pass through the bridging and protocols listed in the coverage register of Section 4. A first-layer predicate with value 0 means only that no witness was found within the declared window, thresholds, upper bounds and protocol class; it does not imply that the corresponding duty fails (Section 9 of the General Form: absence of operationalized evidence does not imply that a duty fails).  
The necessity of the three duties is given by Theorem 1 of the General Form, and the converse direction by its Corollary 1; what the verdicts determine is whether the duties and the fact of constitution hold on the instance. Higher qualifications such as Law, Principle and intention lie outside the adjudication domain.

2\. Upstream Inputs  
*The Ontological Definition of the Sedimentation-Field*: main text of the definition—“A Sedimentation-Field is: the Trace-Response set that signals leave in a medium, together with the mode of organization of this set, transforming under the action of historical dynamics, and its activity.”  
*On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 1 · The General Form of Field Concretization*: the working domain χ = (P, L, B, W) and the active-concretization stipulation; in Section 3, the bearing relation, signals, Trace-Response elements and mode of organization, the organizational condition Cond, action Act, the process-identification rule and component criterion, and the bearing reading of historical dynamics HistCond (“determined in part through an actual material route”); in Section 5, the formal master formula, Theorem 1 and Corollary 1; the Convergence Round lemma (HistCond ⇒ Cond); in Section 7, the in-boundary self-feedback type; in Section 8, the unanswered questions (the action criterion for an indecomposable joint action with zero net change; the general identity condition for modes of organization); in Section 9, “To the adjudication task”: q, r, Part, Act and HistCond are the objects that any adjudication that declares something to realize a Sedimentation-Field must cover; the coverage of the operational predicates is delimited by comparison with Act and HistCond; absence of operationalized evidence does not imply that a duty fails.  
*On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 2 · The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*: D1, the three-valued domain and strong Kleene operations; the F layer of D10–D20 (exact rational arithmetic, ε as the declared resolution, booking only effective components, D15 functional support, D16 Form and Mark, D17 available positions, D18 event atoms, D19 qualification, D20 domain of definition); D9, indecomposable joint events; the 4.2 recursion; Theorem 1 (with only the state trajectory as input, Act and HistCond cannot be decided); Theorem 4 (monotonicity under evidence refinement); Theorem 5 (conditional read-back); 6.3 (the place of the adjudication predicates among the evidence atoms; the bridging is borne by the Adjudication Form); 8.2, read-back antecedents (1) to (6); 8.4 (Q = 0 for a declared T does not imply that no other T satisfies the three duties); 14.2 (indistinguishability under observation protocols belongs to *The Indistinguishability Form of Field Concretization*); the F-layer evaluator of Appendix A.1.  
*The Mathematical Form of the Field* v2.7, Definitions 3, 4, 9: Trace-Response, Structural Position, Sedimentation-Degree.

Canon Chapter 1 v2.2, Part Four, Chapter Eleven (Adjudication Forms).

3\. Declaration of Properties and Observation Set

### 3.1  Adjudication Instance

χ = (P, L, B, W), together with the declaration Δ. Δ is frozen before adjudication; during adjudication no item may be added or deleted, the domain may not be narrowed or replaced, and nothing may be re-declared. Δ contains:  
(1) the descriptive level L and the resolution ε; the inventory 𝒳 of state variables, with the Structural Position of each variable and whether it lies inside or outside the declared boundary;  
(2) the starting point t₀ of the declared period and the starting state s₀: s₀ must be the actual state at t₀ and is checked against records;  
(3) the alphabet of external signals and their entry points (counted only at the boundary), the alphabet including rest; the set 𝓗 of legitimate histories (by default all finite sequences over the alphabet; when constrained by an overall schedule, an input grammar or the like, it must be declared as the language recognized by a finite monitor automaton 𝓜. 𝓗 constrains input histories; the rest windows and measurement tails appended by the adjudication protocol are not included in the check against 𝓗. The witness pair h, h′ of Tr must belong to 𝓗; the actual and reference histories of Hist and Act\_𝓟^h must belong to 𝓗; the legitimacy of a probe p is checked on the extension h·p ∈ 𝓗. The legitimacy of 𝓟 is a global requirement at the level of declaration: for the prospective Act\_𝓟, for every legitimate history h in the search domain (of length at most H\_max), and for the historical Act\_𝓟^h, for the actual and reference histories, every p ∈ 𝓟 must satisfy h·p ∈ 𝓗. This check precedes the witness search and the adjudication of Hist, and is not masked by a 0 from “no witness”. Anything not in 𝓗 gives ⊥\_Dom);  
(4) the schedule of external supply: it may be fixed, and must be independent of the signal history; the actual energy exchange between host and environment may vary with the state;  
(5) the supply cut-off operation (if Tr^cut or Hist^cut is asked): given in the declared controlled model as a legitimate operation, together with its transition law; for a stochastic model declared in the structural representation of 3.3, the cut-off must be given as a structural transformation cut\_φ(s, ω) on the same exogenous disturbance space; a cut-off transition kernel alone does not constitute a twin cut-off protocol, and the cut reading mode then gives ⊥\_Undef;  
(6) the activity observation function act (numerical) and the probe class 𝓟 (with length bound P): 𝓟 is a frozen finite set of probe sequences, each probe nonempty, of length at most P, with symbols in the alphabet, and with the history after its application in 𝓗; 𝓟 does not default to all sequences of length ≤ P over the alphabet—that choice must be written out as a declaration; 𝓟 may be empty;  
(7) parameters: the rest window q, the resolution threshold r (total variation of the joint distribution of in-boundary states, dimensionless), the activity-increment threshold r\_act (in the units of act), the two thresholds r\_μ and r\_ρ of Dep, the upper bound k on the number of positions, and the upper bound H\_max on history length;  
(8) the coupling protocol (required for stochastic models, 3.3);  
(9) the evidence level (Y₁, Y₁ plus Y\_decl, Y₂) and the declared instance protocols (4.2);  
(10) the adjudication mode (3.2).  
Changing the declared boundary yields another adjudication instance χ′, not another reading of the same instance.

### 3.2  Adjudication Modes

Model mode: the object of adjudication is the declared model; the verdict can be 1, 0 or ⊥. Whether the real host conforms to the model falls under Dom4.  
Measurement mode: the object of adjudication is the physical host; paired signal histories are realized with replicas, resettable hosts or populations of the same kind. Only 1 or ⊥ is given, never 0. The statistical conditions for a verdict of 1 are in 4.3.

### 3.3  Clamping and Coupling Protocols

Compare two histories of equal length, h (baseline) and h′ (control); a witness pair is the ordered pair (h, h′).  
Deterministic models (value clamping): after each side makes its own transition, set the out-of-boundary components on the h′ side to those on the h side at the same step; the history segment, rest segment and cut-off segment follow the same rule.  
Stochastic models (twin clamping): Δ must give a structural representation x\_{t+1} = Φ(x\_t, u\_t, ω\_t), with the exogenous disturbance ω declared by component (in-boundary, out-of-boundary) together with its single-beat joint distribution; the ω\_t of different beats are independent and identically distributed. Disturbances correlated across beats (environmental noise with memory, etc.) must be declared as augmented state: the memory of the disturbance is written as state components (out-of-boundary components if outside the declared boundary), driven by innovations independent and identically distributed beat by beat, so that the model still falls within this representation. Where only the single-beat distribution is declared, with no cross-beat structure, adjudication treats the beats as independent; when the actual disturbance is correlated across beats but no augmented declaration was made, the verdict does not extend to that instance (E34). The default legitimate protocol is the twin protocol: at each beat the two sides share all exogenous disturbances of that beat (same-beat sharing, which is distinct from cross-beat independence), and after each transition the out-of-boundary components of the control side are overwritten with the values of the baseline side; μ\_h′ is the distribution of the control side's in-boundary state under the disturbance distribution. In the deterministic case the twin protocol is value clamping.  
A necessary condition for a legitimate protocol: comparing a history with itself gives TV = 0. Here TV is the total variation between the marginal distributions μ\_h and μ\_h′ of the two sides; it and the probability Pr(X ≠ X′) that the samples of the two sides disagree are distinct mathematical objects and are used separately. Without out-of-boundary components no overwriting takes place, each side's marginal depends only on its own transition law and not on the coupling, the self-comparison TV is always 0, and every coupling passes this check; the check then does not identify the coupling. With out-of-boundary components, the control side is overwritten at every step with the baseline side's out-of-boundary values, so the control side's marginal depends on how the disturbances of the two sides are coupled; only then does this check exclude certain couplings.  
Counterexample: in-boundary m\_{t+1} = ω\_t, p\_{t+1} = m\_t, y\_{t+1} = p\_t ⊕ E\_t; out-of-boundary E\_{t+1} = m\_t; the signal enters no variable. On the baseline side p and E both copy the same m, so y ≡ 0; on the control side p copies its own m, while E is overwritten with the baseline side's m, so y = m\_ctl ⊕ m\_base. When the two sides share disturbances, m\_ctl = m\_base, y ≡ 0, and the self-comparison TV = 0. When the control side's disturbances are drawn independently, y is uniformly distributed from the third beat on and the marginal TV is exactly 0.5 (the baseline side being the point distribution at 0); a system that does not respond to signals at all would then be judged Tr = 1. In the same example the disagreement probability Pr(y ≠ y′) is also 0.5, but in general models the two quantities differ: a model without out-of-boundary components has, under independent sampling, marginal TV 0 while its disagreement probability can be 0.5 (Appendix B.1).  
Different legitimate protocols can give different verdicts, so the protocol is a frozen component of Δ. For a stochastic model with no declared legitimate coupling protocol, every verdict involving clamping is ⊥\_Obs. Fixing a random seed does not constitute a legitimate protocol. Self-comparison TV = 0 is only a necessary check of the protocol's self-consistency; it does not prove that the protocol is unique or materially legitimate.  
Supported domain of the adjudication program: finite models with a declared structural representation and exogenous disturbances independent and identically distributed beat by beat (those correlated across beats being declared as augmented state); the paired joint distribution of the two sides is maintained under the twin protocol with shared disturbances, and μ\_h, μ\_h′ are the two marginals of that joint distribution. Models given only by a transition kernel, without a structural representation, can be adjudicated only when the baseline side's out-of-boundary components are point distributions at every step (the overwrite value is then determined, and each side's marginal is the same as under the twin protocol); otherwise they give ⊥\_Obs. Appendix A.1 implements this and runs regressions on four kinds of case: shared sampling on the two sides, independent sampling, random out-of-boundary components, and mixed states (Appendix B.1); in addition, two models with the same single-beat marginal, one independent across beats and one fully correlated across beats, check that the two are not merged: the marginal TV of the history pair (w, w) vs (rest, w) is 0 and 0.5 respectively (Appendix B.1).  
Validity of transition kernels: transition kernels and disturbance distributions must be valid probability kernels—nonempty, with nonnegative probabilities summing exactly to 1 (zero-probability entries are not counted as successors). Invalid ones give ⊥\_Dom and are not treated as valid models. Exact adjudication of a deterministic model further requires that every transition have exactly one successor of positive probability. Exact arithmetic: the adjudication program uses exact rational arithmetic, divided into three separate tasks, each handled at a fixed point. (1) Probability mass: the raw entries of the transition kernel, the cut-off kernel and the exogenous disturbance distribution are first converted entry by entry to rationals (integers and fractions as they stand; a float takes the exact rational value of its shortest decimal representation, e.g. 0.05 becomes 1/20); every entry must be nonnegative and the total must be exactly 1. This check is completed before any key merging, normalization or pushforward through φ, and raw negative entries may not be cancelled out. The exogenous disturbance distribution is checked once when the model is constructed; failing this check makes the declaration invalid, which is recorded at the declaration entry of 4.2, and every public adjudication (enumeration and exact adjudication of Tr, Wr, Hist, Act, Dep, and the report) gives ⊥\_Dom before evaluation, whether or not that distribution would be drawn on under the restricted legitimate histories; no formal 1 or 0 may be given. (2) Effective state representation: the starting point is normalized once when the model is constructed (float components take their exact decimal values); the numerical components of successor states are normalized at the outputs of the transition kernel, the cut-off kernel, φ and cut\_φ, and successors identical after normalization are merged; every run entry—enumeration, exact adjudication, twin protocol, Hist, Act, Dep and the report—starts from this same starting point, and transition laws are always evaluated on normalized states. The values of the exogenous disturbance are inputs to φ and cut\_φ and are not normalized; the net transition, the twin protocol and the cut-off structure all call φ and cut\_φ with the declared original values. (3) Readings and comparisons: act readings, setting levels and the change readings of Dep (pre-state and post-state each normalized before subtraction) are rationals; accumulation of distributions, TV (divided exactly by 2), means, translations and residuals, and the strict comparisons with r, r\_act, r\_μ, r\_ρ never pass through floating point, and strict thresholds are not rewritten with tolerances. Values the model itself produces by floating-point arithmetic are taken as produced: 0.1·3, for example, produces 0.30000000000000004, which is taken at the exact value of that decimal representation and not rewritten as 3/10; comparisons with float literals inside a transition law are evaluated on the normalized rational state. Appendix A.1 checks the exogenous disturbance distribution when constructing the model (recording invalid ones at the declaration entry) and normalizes the starting point; each time it draws on the transition kernel or the cut-off kernel, it first checks the raw probabilities and then normalizes the successor states (Appendix B.1: raw negative probabilities give ⊥\_Dom even after merging or pushforward; when the exogenous disturbance distribution has negative probabilities, does not sum to 1, is empty or has non-finite values, every public adjudication gives ⊥\_Dom both under restricted legitimate histories (only rest*, no distinct history pair) and over all sequences, while a valid distribution gives 0 as usual under rest*; the starting point and disturbance keys are consistent across run entries; sums of 0.8, negative probabilities, empty distributions and a single point with probability 0.3 all give ⊥\_Dom; TV exactly equal to r = 1/3 or 1/7 gives 0, and r slightly below 1/3 gives 1).

### 3.4  Properties

(1) First Layer: Operationalized Predicates  
Let μ\_h be the joint distribution of all in-boundary state variables after applying h from s₀ and then resting q steps (stochastic models per 3.3). All differences are measured by the total variation of joint distributions (Lemma 1).  
Tr^rest (rest retention difference): there exists an ordered history pair (h, h′) ∈ 𝓗², of equal length ≤ H\_max, differing in at most k positions, such that TV(μ\_h, μ\_h′) \> r under clamping.  
Tr^cut (cut-off retention difference): as above, but after resting q steps apply q steps of supply cut-off (clamping as usual), and compare the joint distributions of the declared block.  
Wr (write difference): Tr^rest adjudicated with the current state in place of s₀.  
Hist^rest, Hist^cut (historical retention difference): the actual history h\_act (which must be recorded) and the reference history h\_ref (the examined segment replaced by rest) have TV \> r under clamping after rest (for the cut reading, followed by q steps of supply cut-off). The window-end reading Hist\_W: no rest is added, and the comparison is made at t₁ + 1.  
Act\_𝓟 (in-protocol active-participation witness, prospective form): there exist a witness pair (h, h′) with Tr = 1 under the same reading mode and a p ∈ 𝓟 (only the declared 𝓟 is used, not sequences outside it) such that, under clamping, (a) the difference of act distributions is ≤ r before the probe and \> r after it; or (b) no write by p is detected under the declared window and thresholds (after applying p, both sides complete the checking tail according to the reading mode—for the rest reading, rest q more steps; for the cut reading, cut supply q more steps—and the joint distribution of all in-boundary components has TV ≤ r from that before application; both reading modes check all in-boundary components and do not shrink to the declared block compared by the cut reading), the act distributions of both sides are point distributions, and the absolute difference of the act increments caused by p exceeds r\_act: \|(act after − act before)\_h − (act after − act before)\_h′\| \> r\_act. Clause (b) is a numerical increment predicate; r\_act carries the units of act and is set separately from the dimensionless r. When act is not declared as numerical, clause (b) gives ⊥\_Undef; when act is not a point distribution, clause (b) gives ⊥\_Obs.  
Act\_𝓟^h (historical form): the actual and reference histories with Hist = 1 under the same reading mode take the place of the witness pair.  
Timing of “the same reading mode”: the rest reading is history → rest q steps → probe p → rest q steps (the no-write check); the cut reading is history → rest q steps → cut supply q steps → probe p → cut supply q steps (check). The probe continues from the paired states (for stochastic models, the joint distribution) already processed according to the reading mode; in the cut reading the probe is applied to the post-cut-off state; “before the probe” in clauses (a) and (b) is that state. The reading mode changes only the block compared by Tr and Hist and the timing; the write check of clause (b) uses all in-boundary components under both reading modes. Supply cut-off is an operation separate from inputs; any declared input name (however spelled) is executed only according to its transition law. Appendix A.1 implements this as a phased run, the cut-off steps using internal markers kept separate from the input namespace (Appendix B.1: an instance with rest-reading Act\_𝓟^h = 1 and cut-reading Act\_𝓟^h = 0; with a persistent write outside the declared block, (b) does not hold under the cut reading; an ordinary input named \_\_cut is executed according to its transition law as usual).  
Dep (historical change of the dependence effect): a declared variable pair (x, y), a level set V\_y of y, and a read-out signal u. The V\_y that the program adjudicates in both directions is a frozen finite discrete level set, enumerated completely during adjudication; when V\_y is a continuous interval or an infinite set, an executable membership and setting-legitimacy test must be declared, and every measured level must pass it; levels whose legitimacy cannot be confirmed give ⊥\_Dom; absence of a witness on a finite sample gives only an in-range 0, with the subdomain actually measured reported (the 0 holds only for that subdomain), and no 0 is given for the whole of V\_y; a negative determination over the whole domain requires in addition an analytic-extremum or quantifier-elimination proof with stated premises. The membership test, as a frozen declaration, must have a verifiable identity: a declared identifier and a digest of its implementation (bytecode, constants, names, parameter shape, closure values, positional defaults and keyword-only defaults). The same implementation declared repeatedly has the same identity; different implementations have different identities. When the membership test refers to global values other than built-ins, the digest cannot cover those values and an identifier must be declared; otherwise ⊥\_Dom. In state s, set y = a and y = b (all else unchanged), and let A\_s, B\_s be the distributions of the one-step change of x. The location effect is Δμ(s; a, b) = E\[B\_s\] − E\[A\_s\]; the translation residual is ρ(s; a, b) = TV(B\_s, τ\_{Δμ}A\_s), where τ\_c is translation by c; the effect is D(s; a, b) = (Δμ, ρ). Compare the states s, s̄ (under clamping) at the end of σ of the actual and reference histories that contain the examined segment σ: if there exist a, b ∈ V\_y with \|Δμ(s) − Δμ(s̄)\| \> r\_μ or \|ρ(s) − ρ(s̄)\| \> r\_ρ, the verdict is 1. Δμ carries the units of x and ρ is dimensionless, so the two thresholds r\_μ, r\_ρ are set separately and declared in Δ. Readings and operations follow the exact arithmetic of 3.3. When s or s̄ is not a point distribution, the verdict is ⊥\_Obs.  
Dep₀ (basic dependence effect, single state): in state s, the one-step net change of x differs between y = a and y = b.  
Dep depends on the declared variable pair, level set and coordinates. A “change of the mode of organization” first requires an organizational identity condition; Dep is not to be renamed a change of organization.  
(2) Second Layer: Duties  
T is a declared finite set of Structural Positions, T ⊆ Ξ\_B (D2 of the Mathematical Form). On the declared χ and T:  
q: every element of T is an effective change sustained and retained by a Structural Position, persisting within W; in F, Ret ∧ Form.  
r: the formative history of T contains Trace-Responses left by signals; in F, Src = ⋁\_{i ∈ T} \[Mark(i) ≠ ∅\].  
TraceSet: T ≠ ∅ ∧ q ∧ r, together with cross-time identity Ident and formative-history evidence HistEv; in F, Trace\_F.  
Part: there exists a process π actually occurring within W, with carrier inside B, such that Cond(π, Org(T)); in F, Part\_F.  
Act(ρ): ρ exerts on Org(T) an action effective at L; in F, Act\_F.  
HistCond(ρ): the action of ρ is determined in part, through an actual material route, by Trace-Responses T has already left; in F, HistCond\_F.  
HDyn: there exists ρ with Act ∧ HistCond whose action target belongs to T; in F, HDyn\_F.  
Fld(χ): ∃T \[TraceSet ∧ Part ∧ HDyn\].  
In-boundary self-feedback type (Section 7 of the General Form): there exists ρ ∈ Proc\_W(B) with Act ∧ HistCond such that the route taken by HistCond lies inside B.

### 3.5  Observation Set

(1) Y₁: State-Level Observation  
The operational profile Prof consists of the following components: the partition into in-boundary and out-of-boundary components; s₀; 𝓗 (including the monitor automaton); the alphabet (including rest and probes); the net transition Φ\_t(s, u) of each beat (for stochastic models, the structural representation of 3.3 and the distribution of exogenous disturbances independent and identically distributed beat by beat); the cut-off transition law (if declared); act; the semantics of state setting; the records of the actual history and its trajectory. The frozen operational configuration C\_op is the part of Δ that fixes how questions are asked: q, r, r\_act, r\_μ, r\_ρ, k, H\_max; the probe class 𝓟 and its length bound P; the variable pair, setting level set and read-out input of Dep; the current state asked about by Wr; the actual history and reference segment of Hist; the coupling protocol; the adjudication mode. Prof gives the state-level dynamics and the actual records; C\_op gives the questioning rules by which verdicts are obtained from these dynamics; together they form the input of the first-layer verdicts. C\_op is frozen together with Δ before adjudication and contains no process-by-process mechanism inventory.  
Y₁ is the totality of observations whose outcomes are determined by (Prof, C\_op), including paired signal histories, clamping, setting, probes, records of the actual history, and supply cut-off. All first-layer predicates lie within Y₁ (Theorem 4).  
(2) Y\_decl: Declarations  
May include: the beat-by-beat mechanism inventory 𝓘\_t = {(id, car, tgt, running or not)}; completeness of the inventory; the F-class declaration (host mechanisms separable at L, numerical laws in the three classes constant, linear, bilinear); signal entry positions; single-action-mechanism declarations; read-out carrier declarations.  
(3) Y₂: Mechanism-Level Observation  
KO\_m: at (t, s, u), remove the operation of process m in this beat and read out the post-state; pulses are likewise removed one at a time.  
LOC\_i: set only the state of Structural Position i.  
Out\_m: the read-out of a process declared as a read process.  
External-clamp LOC: apply LOC with the out-of-boundary components clamped and the out-of-boundary carrier processes running as in the baseline.  
(4) Derived Quantities  
Knockout difference: Δ\_m(t, s) := the component of Φ\_t(s) − Φ\_t^{−m}(s) on tgt(m). What is observable is the threshold-truncated booked term, not the full true component: under SEP and MOD, Δ\_m = ĉ\_m(s) := c\_m(s)·\[\|c\_m(s)\| \> ε\] (Theorem 6(1)).  
Legitimate perturbation: for position i, D\_i(s) := {δ ≠ 0 : s(i) + δ ≥ 0, s + δe\_i is a state the declaration permits to be set, and its one-step successor Φ\_t(s + δe\_i) is nonnegative at every position}. A setting whose successor leaves the F domain of definition is not a legitimate perturbation; an applied candidate that is illegitimate shows only that a legitimate response was not obtained, not that D\_i(s) is empty, and gives ⊥\_Obs.  
Perturbation coverage: for each candidate position, four states are distinguished—a legitimate perturbation has been applied (a response obtained); D\_i(s) has been declared empty (the declared setting domain permits no nonzero perturbation); D\_i(s) is nonempty but no perturbation has been applied; the applied candidate is illegitimate (the set value or its one-step successor is negative). Only the first can support a negative determination; the others turn the negative determinations that depend on them into ⊥: the second into ⊥\_Pert, the third and fourth into ⊥\_Obs (no legitimate response obtained; D\_i(s) is not inferred to be empty).  
Available position: a position of T that has s\_t(i) \> ε in the current beat and was formed earlier (D17 of the Mathematical Form).  
Local response: R\_m(i; δ) := \[ĉ\_m(s + δe\_i) ≠ ĉ\_m(s)\], with i an available position and δ ∈ D\_i(s); R\_m(i) := \[∃δ ∈ D\_i(s): R\_m(i; δ)\].  
Closure residual: ρ\_t(x) := Φ\_t(s)(x) − s(x) − Σ(knockout differences of the events in the inventory).

### 3.6  Forward Relation

First layer: each predicate is the computation of the corresponding quantity on the declared model; for stochastic models the forward relation is given by the coupling protocol of 3.3.  
Second layer: 4.2 of the Mathematical Form, s\_{t+1}(i) = s\_t(i) + Σ(effective pulses) + Σ(effective components), with components computed from the pre-beat state; the correspondence between duties and F atoms is given by the correspondence table of Section 9 and by Theorem 5 of the Mathematical Form; the correspondence between F atoms and Y₂ is given by the instance protocols of 4.2 in Section 4.

4\. Construction of the Criteria

### 4.1  Verdicts and Discipline

Det\_X(χ) = (v, Code, Level, Dom, Read), v ∈ {1, 0, ⊥}: Code is the path or reason code; Level is the evidence level used; Dom lists the adjudication-domain conditions and instance protocols used; Read is the reading.  
Reason codes for ⊥:  
⊥\_Obs: insufficient observation (window, resolution, samples, no records, undeclared coupling protocol);  
⊥\_Dom: an adjudication-domain condition fails (s₀ cannot be checked, the inventory is incomplete where a verdict of 0 is called for, etc.);  
⊥\_Undef: a required declaration item is missing (Tr^cut asked without a declared cut-off, Dep asked without a declared variable pair, Act(m) asked without an inventory);  
⊥\_Prof: state-level evidence is insufficient: there exist instances with the same Prof and C\_op on which the duty takes opposite values (Theorem 5);  
⊥\_Inv: the inventory is not declared complete, so no 0 is given;  
⊥\_Sub: a process with no effective component and no faithful read-out, whose read dependency is invisible below the resolution;  
⊥\_Joint: an indecomposable joint process with zero net change (Section 8 of the General Form, D9 of the Mathematical Form);  
⊥\_Id: cross-time identity undetermined;  
⊥\_Rec: formation records missing.  
⊥\_Pert: a declared candidate position has no legitimate nonzero perturbation (D\_i(s) is empty), so the local response required for a negative determination cannot be obtained. This code is not used when the applied candidate is illegitimate but D\_i(s) is not necessarily empty; that case gives ⊥\_Obs.  
These reasons are not lumped together as a vague “insufficient material”.  
Adjudication discipline:  
1\. Finding no positive evidence is not a negative determination;  
2\. A first-layer predicate with value 0 does not imply that a duty is 0;  
3\. While the inventory is not closed, no universal 0 is given;  
4\. ⊥ implies neither direction;  
5\. Conditions propagate only along the bridging and protocols actually used.  
4.2  Adjudication-Domain Conditions and Instance Protocols  
Adjudication-domain conditions (first layer):  
Dom1  Window: q has been declared, and the verdict depends on q (Lemma 5).  
Dom2  Resolution: r has been declared, 0 ≤ r \< 1, and measurement error lies within r; r\_act, r\_μ, r\_ρ are nonnegative. A declaration outside the valid domain gives ⊥\_Dom and may not enter a formal verdict (total variation is at most 1; when r ≥ 1 no difference can exceed r, and computing as usual would yield a 0 with no adjudicative force). In a deterministic model the TV between point distributions takes only the values 0 and 1, so TV \> r is equivalent to “the compared blocks differ and r \< 1”; the adjudication program checks r accordingly (Appendix A.1; the boundary regression at r = 1 is in Appendix B.1). The two thresholds r\_μ, r\_ρ of Dep are declared separately and checked separately (the two-threshold regression of Appendix B.1).  
Dom3  Legitimate intervention: clamping, setting and supply cut-off are given in the declared controlled model as legitimate operations; the physical dynamics after cut-off need not be the same as before it. Setting belongs to testing, not to a history that intrudes on the host. Probes that write must be declared: in a model, the state can be copied; in measurement, the host must be resettable, or the probe must stay below the write threshold.  
Dom4  Faithful model: used only to extrapolate model-mode verdicts to physical hosts.  
Dom5  Inventory and preservation of a verdict of 1: suppose the complete model and the declared model use the same legitimate inputs and intervention mechanisms, and the declared state distribution is indeed a marginal of the complete state distribution; then a detected joint-TV difference of Tr or Hist does not vanish when state variables are added (TV does not increase under marginalization). When an omitted variable affects transitions, clamping or the signal boundary, a verdict of 1 must be rechecked. This clause guarantees only the preservation of the mathematical witness and does not extend to Act\_𝓟 or Dep. A verdict of 0 requires a complete inventory; otherwise ⊥\_Dom.  
Dom6  Checkable starting point: s₀ is checked against records.  
Declaration entry: adjudication first checks the validity of the declaration, then evaluates. First-layer checks: position names nonempty and not repeated; state variables, boundary and initial state of consistent length; alphabet nonempty, without repetition, and containing rest; a transition law present; q a nonnegative integer, k and H\_max positive integers; r conforming to Dom2, every threshold nonnegative; the monitor automaton validly formatted; the transition kernel valid (3.3); the exogenous disturbance distribution valid (3.3; where already checked at construction, the result is inherited here); the cut-off declaration complete (3.1(5)). Mechanism-level checks: position names nonempty and not repeated; the starting state consistent with the position set and nonnegative; ε a nonnegative rational; 1 ≤ t₀ ≤ t₁; T nonempty and within the position set; process names not repeated; for every process, the action target and read positions within the position set, the law of F class with rational parameters, a bilinear law reading exactly two positions, and the running beats not outside the declared window; pulse targets within the position set and amplitudes nonnegative; the input history length equal to the number of beats; perturbation positions within the position set, and each perturbation a legitimate nonzero value or a declared status (no legitimate perturbation; not applied); the process referred to by OUT-f exists (D20 of the Mathematical Form). The mechanism specification agrees with the domain of definition of the upstream evaluator: the read positions of a linear law are nonempty, a constant law carries no read positions, and the declared T has no repetitions; the actual trajectory is generated exactly beat by beat, and any negative position leaves the F domain of definition and gives ⊥\_Dom, with the beat, position and value reported—no truncation to zero, no tolerance. Each call additionally checks the override parameters actually in effect: the given starting point matches the state dimension; every symbol of the input history, reference history and read-out input belongs to the declared alphabet (lists and tuples are synonymous and are represented uniformly after the check); history-segment indices are integers, within the history length, and not repeated; the variable indices of Dep are within the state dimension. The check of default values at model construction does not replace the check at each call; an invalid declaration already found at construction (such as an invalid exogenous disturbance distribution) is inherited by every call before evaluation, and is not exempted from ⊥\_Dom because some evaluation path does not draw on that declaration. Invalid declarations give ⊥\_Dom (an incomplete cut-off declaration gives ⊥\_Undef) and do not enter evaluation, and no program exception is substituted for a verdict. An unsubmitted material certification is not an invalid declaration; it shows up as the ⊥ of the corresponding verdict. validate\_layer1\_decl in Appendix A.1 and validate\_mechanism\_decl in A.4 implement this (Appendix B.1, and C16 of B.4).  
Instance protocols (second layer):  
SEP (separable): the net increment equals the sum of the effective components and effective pulses (4.2 of the Mathematical Form), and the host is of F class.  
MOD (modular knockout): KO\_m removes exactly the component of m; the numerical laws and readings of the other processes are unchanged.  
LOC (local setting, with exclusivity): LOC\_i changes only the value of the state node (i, t), and the value it sets is legitimate; the setting operation does not act directly on the carrier of any process, does not change the numerical law of any process, and transmits nothing to processes via other nodes.  
BEAT (beat alignment): the model beat equals the time resolution of L; components read the pre-beat state (D12 of the Mathematical Form); ε equals the declared resolution of L.  
OUT-f (faithful read-out): for m declared as a read process, at each of its running beats t, Out\_m(t, s) is a position-wise injective encoding g\_m((s(j))\_{j ∈ Rd(m)}) of the values of the positions m actually reads; Rd(m) is the set of positions m actually reads and may be empty. Changing the value of a position actually read necessarily changes Out\_m; changing a position not read does not change Out\_m. In F, an OUT-f process is encoded as a linear law with R(m) = Rd(m) and read certification 1; one with Rd(m) = ∅ is encoded as a zero constant law. Constant outputs, lagged outputs and outputs decoupled from the read positions do not satisfy OUT-f. OUT-f is used only for read processes with no effective component; the read dependency of an effective process is adjudicated by local response (4.6(2)).  
INV-a (truthful declaration): the declared carriers, targets and running status of the processes listed in the inventory are true.  
INV-c (complete inventory): every process and every pulse operating within W is in the inventory.  
REC (record coverage): the records of the actual history cover t₀ − 1 to t₁ and give HistEv.  
ENT (faithful entry): the formative history before the start of the records is registered faithfully as events at the entry beat t₀ − 1—for every position already greater than ε at the start of the records, its formation event, together with its forming agent (signal or in-field continuation) and the out-of-boundary cause, is given as an entry-beat event; or every position of T is no greater than ε at the start of the records. This corresponds to D2 of the Mathematical Form, “the formative history before W is represented in F by the entry beat”; its faithfulness is a declaration item and is not inferred from the records. When ENT is not declared, positions already greater than ε at the start of the records whose Form and Mark show no events give ⊥\_Rec, not 0; criteria that use them as available positions are accordingly undetermined.  
POS (persistence of Structural Positions): T is a set of persistent Structural Positions, and Ident is given. Numerical equality does not imply cross-time identity (7.6 of the Mathematical Form); here identity is given by the persistence of position.  
Structural declarations used by conditional bridging:  
PE (pure pulse entry): signals enter only as pulses added at the declared entry positions, gate no in-boundary process, and there is no out-of-boundary carrier process.  
SAM (single-action mechanism): in that beat the events acting on x consist of exactly one process, and no pulse acts on x.  
AC (read-out carrier): act is the output of a single pure read process inside B, and that process runs within W of the actual history.  
LOCZ (localization of difference): at the comparison beat, the actual and reference histories differ only at positions of T available in that beat.  
IMP (implementation fidelity): local setting preserves the material realization relations at the read end and action end of the original process—what is set is the material variable the original process actually reads, and the action is transmitted along the original host's routes; the response is not obtained via a bypass substitute, injection through a measurement interface, or a change in how the process is implemented. IMP is the instance antecedent for reading back local responses under LOC as actual material routes on χ (8.2(5) of the Mathematical Form); it is not inferred from response values. In model mode the object adjudicated is the declared model, setting directly rewrites the state variables the process reads, and IMP holds by the structure of the model; in measurement mode, and when model verdicts are extrapolated to physical hosts, IMP requires an independent basis of implementation (Dom4).  
The encoding-fidelity conditions EF1–EF5 used for material-level negative determinations are given in Theorem 7(2).  
These are conditions at the level of adjudication method; they are not H and do not enter the definition of any duty.

### 4.3  First-Layer Criteria

Model mode:  
Tr^rest, Tr^cut, Wr: the existence of a witness pair gives 1. When no legitimate history pair of length ≤ H\_max differing in ≤ k positions is a witness, the verdict is 0. For finite deterministic models this 0 has two semantics: when a full-graph search of the pair automaton has been completed, or H\_max is chosen no less than the a priori history-length bound of Theorem 1, the 0 holds for all legitimate finite histories (whole-history 0); a 0 that does not reach whole-history coverage is an in-range 0, holding only for the declared H\_max, k, q and protocol, and may not be written as a whole-history 0. The two kinds of 0 are recorded separately: a model with a long preparatory history gives an in-range 0 at H\_max = 6, while the full-graph search gives 1 with shortest history length 7 (Appendix B.1). An undeclared coupling protocol gives ⊥\_Obs; Tr^cut without a declared cut-off gives ⊥\_Undef, and a model in structural representation lacking cut\_φ(s, ω) likewise gives ⊥\_Undef (3.1(5)).  
Hist: a difference \> r gives 1, ≤ r gives 0; no records gives ⊥\_Obs.  
Act\_𝓟 (prospective, historical): only the declared 𝓟 is traversed. The existence of a witness satisfying (a) or (b) gives 1; no witness within the declared probe class gives 0 (likewise when 𝓟 is empty); an undeclared 𝓟 gives ⊥\_Undef; a probe in 𝓟 that exceeds the length bound or lies outside the alphabet or 𝓗 gives ⊥\_Dom; the historical form further requires the actual and reference histories to belong to 𝓗, otherwise ⊥\_Dom; clause (b) meeting a non-point act distribution gives ⊥\_Obs. Clause (a) checks the TV of act distributions against r, clause (b) checks the increment difference against r\_act; the two thresholds are set separately.  
Dep: the existence of a, b ∈ V\_y with location-effect difference \> r\_μ or residual difference \> r\_ρ gives 1; when V\_y is a finite discrete level set, completely enumerated, and neither difference exceeds its threshold on any a, b, the verdict is 0; when V\_y is infinite, each measured level must pass the declared membership and setting-legitimacy test (otherwise ⊥\_Dom), and absence of a witness gives only an in-range 0, with the measured subdomain reported; measured levels that do not match the declared finite V\_y give ⊥\_Dom; a setting not permitted, or an end state that is not a point distribution, gives ⊥\_Obs; an undeclared variable pair gives ⊥\_Undef.  
A verdict of 0 states s₀, k, H\_max, 𝓟 and the protocol used; its meaning is limited by the qualification boundary of Section 1.  
Measurement mode: a verdict of 1 requires the joint declaration of the null hypothesis H₀: TV(μ\_h, μ\_h′) ≤ r; the test method; error control; and the effect size (the one-sided 1 − α lower confidence bound of TV must exceed r). Only a lower confidence bound \> r gives 1; everything else gives ⊥\_Obs. The lower confidence bound must come from a procedure proved to have the target coverage under the following premises; the name of a method is no guarantee by itself:  
　Sampling units: the replicates on each side are independent sampling units (replicas, independent resets of a resettable host, or individuals drawn independently from a population of the same kind), and the samples of the two sides are mutually independent; replicates correlated through a shared batch, environment or measurement channel are not counted as independent units.  
　Estimation domain: μ takes values on a declared finite reading set (continuous readings must first be discretized by a declared binning, and the verdict is relative to that binning), and the size m of the reading set is frozen before sampling.  
　Coverage validity: the confidence procedure used has proved finite-sample or verified asymptotic coverage under the two premises above. For example, the L1 deviation bound for the empirical distribution on a finite reading set, P(‖μ̂ − μ‖₁ ≥ ε) ≤ (2^m − 2)·exp(−nε²/2) (Weissman, T., Ordentlich, E., Seroussi, G., Verdú, S., Weinberger, M. J. Inequalities for the L1 deviation of the empirical distribution. Hewlett-Packard Laboratories Technical Report HPL-2003-97, 2003, a corollary of Theorem 2.1 under φ(π\_P) ≥ 2), with α/2 allotted to each side to obtain ε₁, ε₂, gives the lower bound TV̂ − (ε₁ + ε₂)/2, whose coverage is no less than 1 − α under i.i.d. sampling. A permutation test further requires the two sides to be exchangeable under H₀; the bootstrap serves only as an asymptotic procedure, and its conditions of applicability must be declared.  
　Search and multiple comparisons: apply a multiple-comparison correction counted over all history pairs actually tested (including adaptively selected pairs); or search on one sample and test only the selected pairs on an independent held-out sample.  
When any of the premises above fails or is undeclared, the verdict is ⊥\_Obs; a 1 so obtained is a statistical conclusion within the protocol. When in vivo clamping is not feasible, Tr gives ⊥\_Obs or switches to model mode.  
Clamping can put the control side into mixed states unreachable under the original dynamics and thereby produce witnesses (Appendix B.1; M1 and M3 of Appendix B.2). Such witnesses show only that the first-layer predicate is 1; whether the control side passes through states unreachable under the original dynamics along the witness trajectory can be marked directly in the pair automaton. Witnesses that depend on mixed states do not count as evidence of action chains (E18).

### 4.4  Coverage Register

The scope within which the first-layer predicates and the second-layer mechanism-level predicates certify the evidence atoms of the Mathematical Form:  
(1) Tr^rest, Tr^cut: certify no atom directly. They are capacity predicates; they ask only about hypothetical history pairs and do not certify the retention of the actual T. Retention in q is read directly from the window-end state (Theorem 5(3)). Tr serves only as corroborating evidence of persistence when the window-end state is unobservable in measurement, and is not promoted to Ret.  
(2) Hist (window-end reading): certifies r under PE and “the signal entry positions within the segment all belong to T” (Lemma 12); without PE it does not certify r (the trace-leaving pair of Theorem 5(1)). Hist = 0 refutes no atom (Lemma 7, and cancellation). The records of the actual history that Hist requires give HistEv.  
(3) Act\_𝓟^h: certifies the Cond of Part under AC and LOCZ when the probe actually occurs within W (Lemma 11). The prospective Act\_𝓟 certifies only capacity, not Part.  
(4) Dep₀: certifies a witness of HDyn under SAM when the net change is effective (Lemma 10).  
(5) Dep: does not certify HistCond. It is candidate evidence of an organizational change at the relational layer; which duty it bears awaits the organizational identity condition. 6.3 of the Mathematical Form lists Dep as a candidate source for HistCond; it is Dep₀ that takes this role, and only under SAM.  
(6) Knockout difference Δ\_m: certifies Act\_F (Theorem 6(1)).  
(7) Local response: certifies functional support and HistCond\_F at available positions, and under LOC exclusivity certifies evidence edges and reads (Theorem 6(2), 4.6).  
(8) Read-out response under OUT-f: certifies the Cond\_F of pure reads in both directions; without OUT-f it certifies only positive responses (Theorem 6(3)).  
(9) Closure residual: a necessary check of inventory completeness (Lemma 8).  
(10) External-clamp LOC: the route condition of the in-boundary self-feedback type (Lemma 13).

### 4.5  Duty Verdicts above the First Layer

Y₁ alone (using only Prof):  
q(x): window-end s(x) ≤ ε gives 0; otherwise ⊥\_Prof.  
r: ⊥\_Prof (Theorem 5(2)).  
Part, HDyn: 0 when every position of T stays no greater than ε within W (the available positions are empty in every twin); otherwise ⊥\_Prof (Theorem 5(2)).  
Act(m), HistCond(m): no inventory, so ⊥\_Undef.  
Fld: ⊥\_Prof. There may be positions outside T that bear Trace-Responses, which Prof alone cannot exclude; so a negative determination of Fld is not available at this level.  
Y₁ plus declarations (the two kinds of completeness are kept apart: position-inventory completeness Dom5 is a declaration of state coverage—the declared state variables exhaust the Structural Positions on χ that could bear Trace-Responses—and does not involve mechanisms; process-inventory completeness INV-c is a mechanism-level declaration and is not used at this level):  
q(x): under the F-class declaration and REC, window-end s(x) \> ε together with s(x) ≤ ε at some beat in the records gives 1; window-end s(x) \> ε with s(x) always greater than ε in the records gives ⊥\_Prof (Theorem 5(3)).  
Fld: under position-inventory completeness (Dom5, i.e., EF1), 0 when no position exceeds ε at window end, or when every position stays no greater than ε within W; otherwise ⊥\_Prof. Proof: Prof gives the state values of the declared positions, and Dom5 makes these positions exhaust the Structural Positions that could bear Trace-Responses. In the first case, every nonempty T has an element no greater than ε at window end, so Ret fails and TraceSet = 0; in the second case, no T has an available position in any beat of W (Avail requires s\_t \> ε), so Cond and HistCond are 0 for every process and Part = 0. Both cases hold for every T, and Theorem 1 of the General Form gives ¬Fld. Only state values and Dom5 are used, not the process inventory. ∎  
r: under PE, 1 by Lemma 12.  
Part: under AC and LOCZ, 1 by Lemma 11.  
HDyn: under SAM and LOC, 1 by Lemma 10.  
None of these gives a 0 excluded by Theorem 5(2).  
4.6  Second-Layer Atom Construction Rules (Y₂)  
Below, m is a process in the inventory and t ∈ W. i is an available position: s\_t(i) \> ε and formed earlier; a position whose formation is undetermined (a starting position without declared ENT) is a “position of undetermined availability”. Each item states the conditions for 1, 0 and ⊥, and notes the antecedents that bear completeness.  
(1) Act\_F(m): \|Δ\_m\| \> ε gives 1; otherwise 0. Antecedents: SEP, MOD. Indecomposable joint processes are not knocked out; with zero net change they give ⊥\_Joint.  
(2) HistCond\_F(m):  
　1: some available position i has R\_m(i) = 1. The certification of the evidence edge (i, t) → m is then recorded as 1, and the read certification as 1. Reason: under LOC exclusivity only node (i, t) is changed, and the booked term of m changes with it, so in F the action of m depends on s(i); this certifies in both directions the functional support and read certification required by D18, as well as the evidence edge of F. Reading this support back as an actual material route on χ from (i, t) to m (8.2(5) of the Mathematical Form) further requires IMP; that confirmation is not inferred from response values alone. F-level certification and material-level read-back are kept apart, and their chains of proof do not borrow from each other.  
　⊥: responses appear only at positions of undetermined availability.  
　⊥: some candidate position (an available position or a position of undetermined availability) has no response obtained—⊥\_Pert if D\_i(s) has been declared empty; ⊥\_Obs if legitimate perturbations exist but none has been applied, or if the applied candidate is illegitimate.  
　0: m is effective, a legitimate perturbation has been applied once at every candidate position, and none responds (Theorem 6(2)(A): Supp\_act is disjoint from these positions, so HistCond\_F = 0 under D18, however the links are declared); or there is no candidate position in the beat. This negative determination does not depend on the closure convention of the evidence-path graph. If an excluded position (one whose formation negation has been certified) responds or was not perturbed, the antecedents of its formation negation are counted in the antecedent list of this 0.  
　⊥: m is ineffective and has no response: ⊥\_Sub. In that case Act\_F(m) = 0, which has no effect on HDyn.  
(3) Cond\_F(m): if m does not run, or the beat has neither available positions nor positions of undetermined availability, 0. If m is effective: the same support and perturbation coverage as in (2), giving 1, ⊥ or 0. If m is ineffective: a positive response gives 1; when OUT-f is declared, adjudication is by the read-out response, and a 0 likewise requires a legitimate perturbation applied at every candidate position; otherwise ⊥\_Sub.  
(4) Part\_F: 1 if some process inside B has Cond\_F = 1; 0 if all are 0 and INV-c is declared; otherwise ⊥ (⊥\_Inv or ⊥\_Sub).  
(5) HDyn\_F: 1 if there exists m with tgt(m) ∈ T, Act\_F = 1 and HistCond\_F = 1; 0 if INV-c is declared and every m has at least one of these equal to 0; otherwise ⊥\_Inv.  
(6) Form(i): any one of a trajectory threshold crossing (Theorem 5(3)), an effective pulse, or an effective component making the position cross the threshold gives 1; when none is seen, 0 if INV-c is declared and either the position is no greater than ε at the start of the records or ENT is declared; ⊥\_Rec if the position is already greater than ε at the start of the records and ENT is not declared; otherwise ⊥\_Inv.  
(7) Mark(i): 1 if an out-of-boundary event declared under INV-a (an out-of-boundary carrier process or an out-of-boundary pulse) has an effective knockout difference at i; when none is seen, 0 under the same antecedents as in (6), otherwise ⊥\_Rec or ⊥\_Inv.  
(8) Ret(i): the window-end state, in both directions.  
(9) Ident: 1 under POS; otherwise ⊥\_Id.  
(10) HistEv: 1 under REC; otherwise ⊥\_Rec.  
(11) Trace\_F = Src ∧ ⋀\_{i ∈ T}(Ret(i) ∧ Form(i)) ∧ Ident ∧ HistEv, Src = ⋁\_{i ∈ T} Mark(i).  
Division of the burden of completeness for negative determinations: the 0 of Act is borne by MOD; the 0 of HistCond and of Cond for effective processes by Theorem 6(2)(A), LOC and perturbation coverage; the 0 of Cond for OUT-f processes by OUT-f; the universal 0 of Part, HDyn, Form and Mark by INV-c; formation and causes before the start of the records by ENT, and those within the records by REC; the closure convention of the evidence-path graph bears no negative determination in this reconstruction.  
Antecedent list of a negative determination: every verdict of 0 carries the set B of declared antecedents it consumes (drawn from PROTO, INV-c, REC, ENT, POS, EF4, OUT-f; PROTO denotes SEP, MOD, LOC, BEAT, INV-a collectively). Atoms: the 0 of Act has {PROTO}; the 0 of HistCond and of Cond for effective processes has {PROTO}, united with the formation-negation antecedents of excluded positions that respond or were not perturbed; the 0 of Cond for OUT-f processes also contains OUT-f; the 0 of Form and Mark has {PROTO, INV-c, REC}, plus ENT for positions already greater than ε at the start of the records; the 0 of Ret has the empty set (using only Y₁). Composites: when a conjunction is 0, take the intersection of the lists of its 0 conjuncts; when a disjunction is 0, take the union; when the disjunctions of Part and HDyn are 0, INV-c is added as well. Undeclared antecedents are all treated as failing and never defaulted to true. The load-bearing character of antecedent lists is shown in Lemma 6.1.  
Item-by-item bearing of the read-back antecedents of 8.2 of the Mathematical Form:  
(1) Trace-Responses: Form and Mark are given by knockout differences and threshold crossings, with effectiveness borne by BEAT.  
(2) Retention and identity: Ret by the window-end state, Ident by POS, HistEv by REC; formative history before the start of the records by ENT.  
(3) Individuation, carrier and target: INV-a; universal negative determinations further require INV-c.  
(4) Organizational condition and action: SEP, MOD, LOC; pure reads further require OUT-f.  
(5) Source: local responses under LOC exclusivity, with evidence edges certified as in (2).  
(6) Encoding fidelity: positive determinations need only that the witnesses obtained be genuine, which is given by (1) to (5) and the F-class declaration; negative determinations require universal encoding fidelity (Theorem 7(2)).

### 4.7  The Fld Verdict

Under Y₂ let T\_max := {x ∈ I : s\_{t₁+1}(x) \> ε ∧ Form(x) ≠ ∅},  
　　Det\_Fld(χ) := Q\_F(T\_max), with Det\_Fld := 0 when T\_max = ∅.  
Antecedents of a verdict of 1 (F-model verdict): SEP, MOD, LOC, BEAT, INV-a, POS, REC; when pure reads bear Part, OUT-f as well. Reading back Fld for a material instance further requires IMP and Dom4 (Theorem 7(1)); in model mode IMP holds by the structure of the model.  
Antecedents of a verdict of 0: EF1–EF5 in addition (Theorem 7(2)); when these are not met, wherever 0 would otherwise be given the verdict becomes ⊥ (⊥\_Inv or ⊥\_Dom).  
When some retained position at window end has Form = ⊥, T\_max is undetermined: compute Q\_F(T₁) on the set T₁ of retained positions with Form = 1; if its value is 1 the verdict is 1 (the existence of a witness suffices); otherwise ⊥\_Inv.  
4.8  The Verdict on the In-Boundary Self-Feedback Type  
Suppose ρ has its carrier inside B, and 4.6 gives Act\_F(ρ) = 1 and HistCond\_F(ρ) = 1 (via available position i). If R\_ρ(i) = 1 under external-clamp LOC, the verdict is 1; otherwise ⊥ (Lemma 13).  
Sufficient condition for a verdict of 0: INV-c and EF4 are declared, the atom-adjudication protocols of 4.6 (SEP, MOD, LOC, BEAT, INV-a) hold, and every process inside B that runs within W has Act\_F = 0 or HistCond\_F = 0 (as adjudicated values). INV-c gives only the completeness of the process inventory; the Act and HistCond of each process are adjudicated by 4.6 under the listed protocols; EF4 makes the F encoding cover their mode of material bearing, excluding cases where Trace-Responses determine occurrence, timing or position without appearing as same-beat functional support. Reason: this type requires a process with Act ∧ HistCond in Proc\_W(B); INV-c gives the whole of Proc\_W(B), and EF4 makes HistCond\_F = 0 in F read as a negation at the object level, so there is no candidate in the domain of the quantifier. ∎  
In all other cases the verdict is ⊥. In particular: when some process has Act\_F = 1 and HistCond\_F = ⊥, 0 cannot be inferred from “there is no certified doubly true process”; when some process has Act\_F = HistCond\_F = 1 but no response under external-clamp LOC, it cannot be inferred that its route does not lie entirely inside B (Lemma 13 gives only the positive direction), and the verdict is ⊥.  
selfloop\_noext in Appendix A.4 is a restricted implementation of this verdict: it is used only for models with no out-of-boundary components and no out-of-boundary pathways, where external-clamp LOC coincides with LOC; instances with out-of-boundary pathways must carry out the external-clamp intervention, otherwise ⊥.

### 4.9  Order of Adjudication and Report

Order of adjudication: first-layer predicates → atoms (4.6) → the three duties → Fld. First-layer verdicts do not block the second layer: the second-layer criteria do not presuppose a first-layer verdict of 1.  
Report: J(χ) = (first-layer verdicts; the verdicts on q, r, TraceSet, Part, Act, HistCond, HDyn, Fld; the evidence levels and protocols used; open items), each item written as the five-tuple of 4.1; every verdict of 0 additionally gives its antecedent list (4.6). The report has three layers: observational facts (trajectories, knockout differences, local responses, read-outs); declarations and their certification status; verdicts.  
Scope of the appendix programs: the adjudicators of Appendix A give values; A.1 and A.4 each have a five-tuple report adapter (report) that writes each public verdict as Det(v, Code, Level, Dom, Read). The Code given by the adapter is a summary of the adjudication path: for 1, a witness; for 0, an in-range 0, a full-graph-search 0, or the antecedent list; for ⊥, the reason code. Level is filled in by evidence level. Dom records the configuration actually in effect: in the first layer, the signature of the adjudication function uniformly binds positional arguments, keyword arguments and defaults, and Dom records all parameters of C\_op, the starting point s₀, the coupling protocol and 𝓗 (a monitor automaton is recorded by a frozen digest; the membership test of an infinite setting domain by its identifier and implementation digest), so that the same set of parameters gives the same Dom however it is passed; in the second layer, Dom records the declared protocols and antecedents, together with T, ε, the declared period, the input history, the perturbation settings and the test bench; reports on individual atoms (Form, Mark, Act, Cond, HistCond and the in-boundary self-feedback type) are taken from the result of one and the same adjudication run and carry the same configuration, Read records the kind, the position or process name and the beat index, and when the given result contains no such atom the verdict is ⊥\_Undef. For exact adjudication, Read lists separately the search-derived upper bound on history length (N and I′ being obtained by this exhaustive search; N + N·\|I′\|·k without monitoring, multiplied further by M\_c² with monitoring), the number of expanded nodes searched, and the a priori history-length bound; the latter must be given from the declared upper bound on N and the in-boundary state space \|S\_in\| (Theorem 1 (a2)), and when undeclared is recorded as not provided; the search-derived bound is not passed off as the a priori bound used to choose H\_max. Read gives the witness, the history length and search bounds, the measured subdomain, or the evidence edges. When the declaration entry fails, the report has v = ⊥ and Code = ⊥\_Dom, does not enter the search, and writes no formal 1 or 0.  
Coverage of report items by the reference programs:  
　Value v (first and second layers): given by A.1 and A.4, within the supported domain listed in 3.3.  
　Validity of the declaration entry: checked by A.1 and A.4 (invalid gives ⊥\_Dom).  
　Antecedent list B of second-layer 0 verdicts: output automatically by A.4.  
　Reason codes for ⊥: in the first layer ⊥\_Obs, ⊥\_Undef, ⊥\_Dom; in the second layer ⊥\_Prof, ⊥\_Pert, ⊥\_Obs, ⊥\_Sub, ⊥\_Inv, ⊥\_Rec, ⊥\_Id, ⊥\_Dom; generated automatically by A.1 and A.4 (Appendix B.1, and C17 of B.4); ⊥\_Joint does not occur in this model class and is not generated automatically.  
　Form of the five-tuple: report in A.1 and A.4 outputs the five fields automatically (Appendix B.1, and C17 of B.4).  
　Complete derivation path of Code (atom-by-atom evaluation): written out by the rules of Section 4; the adapter gives only a summary.  
　Conformity of Level and Dom with the instance: the adapter records the configuration actually in effect; whether the declared protocols and antecedents are true of the instance is adjudicated by the rules of Section 4 and is not given by the adapter.  
　Material-level read-back qualification (the antecedents of 8.2, IMP and EF1–EF5 holding on the physical object): not generated by any model program.  
The reference programs cover the first five items; the remaining items are not given by them.

5\. Proof of the Validity of the Criteria

### 5.1  First Layer

Lemma 1 (the metric must use joint distributions; invariance under blockwise maps)  
(i) Two bits (a, b): signal eq makes (a, b) uniform over (0, 0), (1, 1); signal ne makes it uniform over (0, 1), (1, 0). Every single-variable marginal is uniform, so a marginal criterion gives 0; the joint TV = 1.  
(ii) Let f = f\_in × f\_ext be a blockwise bijection, with the clamping, cut-off, probe and other intervention protocols conjugated along with f, and with the declared block preserved for Tr^cut. Then the values of Tr^rest, Tr^cut, Wr and Hist are invariant under f; when act is composed with f, Act\_𝓟 is invariant as well.  
Proof: the total variation of joint distributions is invariant under bijections; clamping involves only the out-of-boundary block. Dep depends on coordinates and lies outside the scope of invariance. ∎  
Lemma 1.1 (the acceptance predicate is determined by the node)  
The nodes of the pair automaton are (s, s′, j): s is the baseline-side state, s′ the control-side state after clamping, and j the number of differing positions used so far. Tail operator Tail(s, s′): apply q rest steps to each side (when reading Tr^cut, a further q supply cut-off steps), clamping at every step according to 3.3. Acceptance predicate Acc(s, s′, j) := \[j ≥ 1\] ∧ \[the two sides of Tail(s, s′) differ on the compared block\]. Acc is a function of the node and does not depend on the path by which the node is reached.  
Proof: The model is deterministic, rest and supply cut-off do not read the input history, and the clamping rule depends only on the current states of the two sides; hence Tail is a function of (s, s′). j is a component of the node. ∎  
Theorem 1 (decidability and history-length bounds for finite deterministic models)  
Let the model be deterministic with finitely many states, and let N be the number of states reachable from s₀ via signal transitions (including rest).  
(a1) With no out-of-boundary variables and 𝓗 the set of all finite sequences: if a Tr^rest (or Tr^cut) witness pair exists, then a witness pair of history length \< N²(k + 1) exists.  
(a2) With out-of-boundary variables: let I′ be the set of values taken by the in-boundary component of the control side at nodes with j ≥ 1 in the paired run; then a witness pair of history length \< N + N·\|I′\|·k exists. The a priori bound used for choosing H\_max in advance is N + N·\|S\_in\|·k (S\_in the declared in-boundary state space). N²(k + 1) does not hold here.  
(b) For any H and any q ≥ 1 there is a model without out-of-boundary variables, with 2(H + 1) reachable states, in which no history pair of length ≤ H is a witness while one of length H + 1 is (for q = 0, (a, rest) is already a witness). Hence any fixed bound that does not grow with the model yields false 0 verdicts.  
(c) When 𝓗 is recognized by a monitor automaton 𝓜 with M states: the pair nodes extend to (s, s′, j, m, m′), where m and m′ are the monitor states of the two histories, and acceptance additionally requires m and m′ both to be accepting states. The number of extended nodes is bounded by B\_c = M²·B (B the node bound of (a1) or (a2)). If 𝓗 cannot be recognized by a finite monitor automaton, the theorem does not apply and a 0 verdict is relative to H\_max. Both the exact decision and the enumeration in Appendix A.1 accept a monitor automaton: the exact decision merges the monitor states into the pair nodes and additionally requires both monitor states to be accepting; the enumeration uses only histories accepted on both sides. A shortest accepting path of the pair automaton repeats no node; since the monitor states are in the nodes, the input string remains legitimate after cycles are deleted. Regression in Appendix B.1: when the all-sequence language has a witness but 𝓗 forbids that witness, the verdict is 0; when the shortest all-sequence witness is forbidden, a legitimate longer witness is returned. If no monitor automaton is declared, adjudication uses the all-sequence language.  
Hence Tr^rest and Tr^cut are decidable in finite deterministic models (with 𝓗 all sequences or regular).  
Degeneracy and counting conventions: the initial node (s₀, s₀, 0) has j = 0 and is never accepting; a witness history has length at least 1. If k = 0, or the paired search has no reachable node with j ≥ 1 (in which case I′ = ∅), or 𝓜 accepts no nonempty history, then no witness exists; Tr = 0 follows directly from completion of the search, and the history-length bounds hold vacuously in this case and are not used. A path of length L passes through L + 1 nodes; with no repeated node, L + 1 ≤ the number of actually reachable nodes ≤ the node bound, hence L \< the node bound. The theorem is governed by the number of actually reachable nodes; the bound serves only to choose H\_max in advance. With a constrained 𝓗, an accepting node must also belong to the accepting set of the extended automaton.  
Proof:  
An ordered history pair (h, h′) is a witness if and only if the node reached from (s₀, s₀, 0) by stepping along h and h′ (at each step each side takes its input; j increases by one if the inputs differ; the control side's out-of-boundary component is set to the baseline side's) satisfies Acc. The history length is the path length; it counts input steps only, not the tail.  
By Lemma 1.1, if an accepting path exists, deleting its cycles still reaches the same accepting node; so there is an accepting path with no repeated node, and its length is less than the number of nodes. The input string after cycle deletion must be legitimate: this holds automatically when 𝓗 is all sequences; when 𝓗 is constrained, the monitor states have been merged into the nodes, the two ends of a cycle have the same monitor state, and the string after cycle deletion is still accepted by 𝓜.  
Counting (same state encoding): when j = 0, the two sides have had identical inputs so far, s = s′, and s lies in the reachable set; nodes ≤ N. When j ≥ 1, s still lies in the reachable set (the baseline side is never clamped); the out-of-boundary component of s′ always equals that of s (clamping at every step), so the node is determined by (s, s′\_in, j) with s′\_in ∈ I′; nodes ≤ N·\|I′\|·k. I′ is computed from the clamped paired run, not as an independent reachable set of the original model. With no out-of-boundary variables, s′ is reached by the original dynamics and s′\_in lies in the reachable set; nodes ≤ N + N²k ≤ N²(k + 1).  
Witness M1 for the failure of N²(k + 1) in (a2): state (m, E), m ∈ {0..12} in-boundary, E ∈ {0, 1} out-of-boundary, s₀ = (0, 0), alphabet {w, a, rest}; w sends (m, E) → (1, 1); otherwise, if m ≠ E, a sets m ← min(12, m + 1), and rest with m \< 12 sets m ← E; everything else is unchanged. q = 2, k = 1. Under the original dynamics N = 2 states are reachable: {(0, 0), (1, 1)}. At the first step clamping turns the control side from (1, 1) into (1, 0); thereafter it increments on the mixed states (2..12, 0), which the original dynamics cannot reach. For q ≥ 1, a state whose m has not yet reached 12 returns to E as soon as it rests, so m must first be pushed to 12. The shortest witness is the ordered pair (a¹², w·a¹¹), of history length 12 \> N²(k + 1) = 8; the paired search has 17 nodes, \|I′\| = 13, and the bound N + N·\|I′\|·k = 28 ≥ 12. Both implementations agree with brute-force enumeration over history lengths ≤ 13 (Appendix B.1, B.2).  
Proof of (b) (model M2, n = H): state (c, x), both in-boundary, c ∈ {0..n}, x ∈ {0, 1}, s₀ = (0, 0), alphabet {a, w, rest}: a sets c ← min(n, c + 1); w with c ≥ n sets x ← 1; everything else (w with c \< n, or rest) sets c ← 0. The ordered pair (a^{n+1}, aⁿ·w) is a witness; for history length ≤ n, x cannot be written, and c is cleared after rest. For n = 6: N = 14, no witness of history length ≤ 6, a shortest witness of history length 7, and 138 nodes in the paired search (Appendix B.1, B.2). The number of reachable states is 2(n + 1). This model has no out-of-boundary variables; it does not test clamping and serves only for the lower bound on history length.  
The gap between the upper and lower bounds is not proved tight. Stochastic models have no such result; a 0 verdict is relative to H\_max. ∎  
Theorem 2 (the position-count bound and its failure under clamping)  
(a) Premises: the comparison is unclamped (no out-of-boundary variables, or only the in-boundary block is compared without clamping); 𝓗 is closed under position-by-position substitution; every history on the chain is subjected to the same rest and measurement conditions. Conclusion: suppose h and h′ differ in m positions and TV(μ\_h, μ\_h′) = d \> r. If r \> 0, let n = ⌈d/r⌉ − 1 ≥ 1 and b = ⌈m/n⌉; then there is a legitimate history pair differing in ≤ b positions with TV \> r. If r = 0, there is a legitimate history pair differing in only 1 position with TV \> 0.  
Proof: Without clamping each history has a determinate distribution on the compared block, and TV is a pseudometric on 𝓗. Substituting position by position gives a chain of length m, every history on which is legitimate by closure under substitution; divide it into n blocks of ≤ b positions each. The triangle inequality gives d ≤ the sum of the TVs between the endpoints of the blocks, so some block has TV \> r. For r = 0, take n = m with 1 position per block: d \> 0 and d ≤ the sum of the TVs of adjacent history pairs, so some adjacent pair has TV \> 0. ∎  
(b) Under clamped comparison the position-count bound fails in general. Counterexample M3: state (m, E), m in-boundary, E out-of-boundary, both in {0, 1}, s₀ = (0, 0), alphabet {a, b, rest}, 𝓗 all sequences, q = 1. Transitions:  
　(0,0): a → (0,0), b → (1,0), rest → (0,0);  
　(0,1): a → (0,1), b → (0,0), rest → (0,0);  
　(1,0): a → (0,1), b → (0,0), rest → (0,1);  
　(1,1): a → (1,0), b → (0,1), rest → (1,1).  
From s₀ the original model reaches {(0,0), (0,1), (1,0)}, N = 3; (1,1) appears only on the control side after clamping.  
　Clamped, k = 1: the pair automaton exhausts all 8 nodes and finds no accepting node; there is no witness of any history length;  
　Clamped, k = 2: the ordered pair (h, h′) = (ba, ab) is a witness; the reversed ordered pair is not.
