Baseline: (0,0) →b→ (1,0) →a→ (0,1) →rest→ (0,0); control: (0,0) →a→ (0,0) →b→ (1,0), the out-of-boundary component set to 1 gives (1,1) →rest→ (1,1), and the out-of-boundary component set to 0 gives (1,0); the final in-boundary components are 0 and 1. The pair differs in m = 2 positions with d = 1; with r = 0.05, the form of (a) would require a witness with b = 1 position, and none exists;  
　Unclamped, k = 1 and k = 2: the pair automaton exhausts the full graph; there is no witness of any history length, in agreement with (a).  
Reason: clamped comparison takes the baseline as its reference and is asymmetric; adjacent history pairs on the chain are each clamped to a different baseline and share no common response map, so the triangle inequality cannot be applied. Both implementations agree with brute-force enumeration (Appendix B.1, B.2).  
(c) For any k there is a model in which no history pair with ≤ k positions is a witness while one with k + 1 positions is.  
Model: x ∈ {0, 1}, s₀ = 0, alphabet {w, rest}; when x = 0, w sets x to 1 with probability ε₀; everything else leaves x unchanged; no out-of-boundary variables.  
Step 1 (upper bound): for any coupling (X, Y) of μ and ν, TV(μ, ν) ≤ P(X ≠ Y). For any legitimate history pair differing in j positions, construct a coupling: the two sides share the same uniform random number U\_t at each step, and x is set when “x = 0, the input is w and U\_t \< ε₀”. At positions with the same input the two sides behave identically; if the final states differ, then at some differing position one side applied w, U\_t \< ε₀ at that step, and that side had x = 0 at that time. Hence P(X ≠ Y) ≤ 1 − (1 − ε₀)^j, and so TV ≤ 1 − (1 − ε₀)^j.  
Step 2 (attaining the bound): take the history pair (w^j, rest^j). The final state on the rest side is always 0, and the w side is set with probability 1 − (1 − ε₀)^j; the TV between the two final-state distributions is exactly this value.  
By the two steps, the maximum TV over history pairs with ≤ k positions is 1 − (1 − ε₀)^k; taking r ∈ \[1 − (1 − ε₀)^k, 1 − (1 − ε₀)^{k+1}) gives the conclusion. Exact computation: ε₀ = 0.05; for j = 1, 2, 3, TV = 0.0500, 0.0975, 0.1426; with r = 0.08, k = 1 has no witness and k = 2 has one (Appendix B.3). ∎  
(d) The positional reading and the contiguous-segment reading are not equivalent.  
Model (gated clock): state (c, x), c ∈ {0..3} incremented by one at every step (mod 4), x ∈ {0..3}; when c = 0, w increments x by one with probability 0.04 (up to 3), and inputs at other positions do not affect x; s₀ = (0, 0), q = 1, r = 0.05.  
Positional reading: applying w at two positions with c = 0 (span 5) gives, against all-rest, TV = 1 − 0.96² = 0.0784 \> r; this is a witness for k = 2.  
Universal proof for the contiguous-segment reading (any history length, any legitimate baseline). Premises: (i) the clock phase c is advanced only by time and is not changed by inputs; (ii) the two histories start from the same initial state; (iii) the stochastic transitions of the two sides share random numbers as in (c); (iv) saturation of x can only make some success leave x unchanged and does not increase the probability of a difference. By (i) and (ii), any contiguous segment of length ≤ 4 contains at most one position with c = 0; x is affected by inputs only at positions with c = 0, so a history pair differing only within such a segment has at most one position with a differing opportunity to write. By the coupling inequality of Step 1 of (c) together with (iii) and (iv), TV ≤ 0.04 ≤ r, so the pair is not a witness. A segment of length 5 can contain two windows.  
Hence a witness with “≤ 2 positions” exists, while a witness with “a contiguous segment of length ≤ 4” does not. Exact computation for history length ≤ 9 with the all-rest baseline agrees (Appendix B.3); that computation serves only as regression and does not replace the universal proof. Tr uses the positional reading. ∎  
Lemma 2 (in-boundary dependency chains)  
(i) If the signal difference is handed to the out-of-boundary part at every step and no copy remains in-boundary (including mutual write-back between in-boundary and out-of-boundary components), then after clamping there is no in-boundary difference; Tr does not return 1, and neither does Act\_𝓟.  
(ii) Retention condition for an in-boundary copy: suppose there are an in-boundary component c and a legitimate history pair (h, h′) (with at most k positions and length at most H\_max) such that the marginal distributions of c in the final states of h and h′ differ with TV \> r, and c is maintained by the in-boundary part itself under rest, i.e. in the rest transition the next value of c equals its current value and does not depend on other components. Then Tr^rest = 1.  
Proof: (i) Clamping overwrites the control side's out-of-boundary components with the baseline side's values at every step; if at every step the difference resides only out-of-boundary, then after overwriting the in-boundary distributions of the two sides are the same, and they remain the same after the rest window. Act\_𝓟 presupposes a Tr witness and likewise does not return 1. (ii) Clamping overwrites only out-of-boundary components, and the rest update of c depends only on c itself and is the identity; so after q rest steps the marginal distributions of c on the two sides are the same as before rest, and their difference is still \> r. The TV of the in-boundary joint distribution is not less than the TV of its marginal c, so TV \> r and (h, h′) is a witness. ∎  
The retention condition cannot be dropped. In a model with only the in-boundary position m, where input w sets m = 1 and rest sets m = 0, a copy remains in-boundary after w but is cleared by a single rest step; exact adjudication gives Tr = 0 (Appendix B.1). Here c = m is not maintained under rest and falls outside the premises of (ii). A copy in mirrored storage is maintained under rest; exact adjudication gives Tr = 1.  
Witness M4 for (i): state (m, E), m in-boundary, E out-of-boundary, both in {0, 1}, s₀ = (0, 0), alphabet {w, rest}: first m′ = E, E′ = m; if u = w, then additionally m′ = 1. q = 4, k = 1: unclamped, Tr = 1 with shortest witness (w, rest); clamped, Tr = 0, the pair automaton exhausts all 12 nodes, and brute force up to history length 10 agrees (Appendix B.1, B.2). ∎  
Lemma 3 (a sufficient condition for Dep = 0)  
Suppose that on the two final states s, s̄, for every a, b ∈ V\_y in question, B\_s = τ\_c A\_s and B\_s̄ = τ\_c A\_s̄, with the same shift c = c(a, b) in both states. Then D(s; a, b) = D(s̄; a, b) = (c, 0), and Dep reports no difference on that pair.  
Proof: When B = τ\_c A, Δμ = c and ρ = TV(τ\_c A, τ\_c A) = 0. ∎  
The lemma uses only the shift relation on the two final states and the equality of the shifts; the background distributions A\_s and A\_s̄ may differ. Witnesses: the receiver-saturation model gives Dep = 1; the linear additive model gives Dep = 0; a stochastic final state gives ⊥\_Obs (Appendix B.1).  
Lemma 4 (Wr and Hist are separate)  
Take two instances with the same current state, one written by a signal and the other set as its initial value: a criterion that reads only the current state cannot make Hist valid in both directions. ∎  
Lemma 5 (the window cannot be eliminated)  
For any q there is a model with slow leakage of the medium whose Tr^rest and Tr^cut are 1 under q and 0 under a longer window. Witness: charge leaking with probability 0.02 per step gives 1 at q = 6 and 0 at q = 300 (Appendix B.1). ∎  
Lemma 6 (what the supply cut-off test measures)  
Model with refresh-maintained charge (held by the supply during rest, lost on supply cut-off): Tr^rest = 1, Tr^cut = 0; Hist^rest = 1, Hist^cut = 0 (Appendix B.1). Tr^cut measures state retention under removal of the supply; it is an additional tolerance test. Retention under supply cut-off has not been proved to be a necessary condition of Trace-Response qualification, nor is it a general dividing line between medium Trace-Responses and non-medium Trace-Responses: states that are formed by history under normal operating conditions and constrain subsequent activity (such as synaptic states maintained by metabolism) may vanish under supply cut-off, and supply cut-off may also alter the medium under test itself. ∎  
Lemma 7 (the counterfactual character of Hist)  
Hist adjudicates the counterfactual difference of the actual history relative to the declared reference history. If the host has a redundant formation route (the reference history reaches the same state by another route), Hist = 0 does not imply that the tested segment left no effective change in the actual history.  
Witness (redundant formation): a single unit x with initial value 0; inputs a and b both write x to 1, and rest maintains it. The actual history (a, b) and the reference history (rest, b) both end with x = 1, so Hist = 0; yet in the actual history the first beat, a, changes x from 0 to 1, which is an effective change (Appendix B.1). It follows from the definition and the actual events that a final-state difference is not a necessary condition for an actual trace. The conclusion of this lemma is limited to this: Hist = 0 is not to be read as the absence of a trace. It does not supply a general contribution-attribution protocol. ∎  
Theorem 3 (validity of the first-layer criteria)  
Under Dom1–Dom3, Dom6 and the declared s₀, k, H\_max, 𝓟, V\_y and coupling protocol, in model mode each first-layer verdict is 1 if and only if the corresponding operationalized predicate holds in the declared model. In finite deterministic models, a 0 verdict for Tr holds for all legitimate finite histories when the full-graph search has been completed or H\_max is not less than the bound of Theorem 1 (Theorem 1), and otherwise holds only for the declared H\_max, k, q (4.3). In measurement mode, the validity of a 1 verdict is as in 4.3.  
Proof: Each criterion is the computation of the operationalized predicate in the model; the universality of a 0 verdict comes from Theorem 1. A 0 for Act\_𝓟 holds only for the declared 𝓟; a 0 for Dep holds for all of V\_y only when V\_y is a completely enumerated finite set, and is otherwise an in-range 0 (4.3). ∎  
This theorem concerns validity at the definitional level. The adjudication program returns 1 or 0 only after the declaration entry of 4.2 has been passed; invalid declarations (including invalid override parameters, probes and history segments) return ⊥\_Dom and do not enter the verdicts this theorem speaks of. On stochastic models it holds within the supported domain of 3.3: models that declare a structural representation and beat-wise i.i.d. disturbances (cross-beat correlated ones declared as augmented states) are computed under the twin protocol; models that give only a transition kernel are limited to the case where the baseline side's out-of-boundary component is a point distribution, return ⊥\_Obs otherwise, and do not claim the complete protocol. Stochastic clamping protocols other than the twin protocol, and statistical procedures for measurement mode, have not been certified for the adjudicator. This theorem does not extend to duty qualification.  
5.2  Adjudication Limits of the First Layer  
Theorem 4 (profile invariance under a fixed operational configuration)  
Under a jointly frozen valid operational configuration C\_op, the value of each first-layer verdict (Tr^rest, Tr^cut, Wr, Hist, Act\_𝓟, Act\_𝓟^h, Dep, Dep₀) is a functional of (Prof, C\_op): if two instances agree in both Prof and C\_op, every verdict agrees.  
Proof: The definitions of the verdicts use only net transitions (including rest, supply cut-off and probe inputs), the clamping rule (which involves only the partition into in-boundary and out-of-boundary components), state setting, act and records, which are components of Prof; and the thresholds, the window, the search ranges of position count and history length, the probe class, the variable pair and level set of Dep, the reference history and the coupling protocol, which are components of C\_op. For every verdict the computational inputs of the two instances are identical, and so are the outputs. ∎  
Prof alone does not determine the verdicts. For one and the same stochastic write transition kernel (x initially 0; w sets x to 1 with probability 0.05 when x = 0; rest maintains), with H\_max = 1 and k = 1, r = 0.01 gives Tr = 1 and r = 0.08 gives 0; with r = 0.08 and H\_max = 2, k = 1 gives 0 and k = 2 gives 1 ((w, w) vs (rest, rest), TV = 0.0975) (Appendix B.1). Hence, from Section 5 on, whenever two instances with “the same Prof” are compared, they are compared under a common C\_op.  
Theorem 5 (adjudication limits of the first layer)  
(1) No two-way adjudication. There exist five pairs of class-F mechanism models ((a), (b), (c), (d), (f) below) such that, under a jointly frozen C\_op, the Prof of each pair is identical (ε = 0; the net-increment polynomials coincide beat by beat and input by input; the trajectories of all input histories, the net transitions at sampled states and Dep₀ all coincide), while the following components differ:  
(a) Cancelling pair: one side has only a pure-read process within W; the other additionally has two Trace-Response-dependent processes +x/2 and −x/2. Act and HDyn differ.  
(b) Source pair: one side writes +1/2 by a constant law; the other has two processes, x/2 and 1/2 − x/2. HistCond and HDyn differ.  
(c) Trace-leaving pair: on one side an amplification +x/2 with out-of-boundary carrier cancels an in-boundary shallowing −x/2; on the other side the signal merely stops the in-boundary shallowing. The window-end reading of Hist is 1 on both sides; r differs.  
(d) Carrier pair: the carrier of the read-out process lies inside B on one side and outside the boundary on the other. Part differs.  
(e) On the four pairs (a)–(d), take in addition ∃T Q: ∃T Q also differs on each pair, so Fld differs. (e) is not a sixth pair of models.  
(f) Formation pair: the position stays above ε throughout the record; one side receives a signal pulse, the other an input-gated in-boundary constant law, and a cancelling out-of-boundary/in-boundary pair is added so that r holds on both sides. The formation record of q differs.  
By Theorem 4, under a common C\_op any verdict that uses only first-layer criteria gives the same output on both members of each pair, so it cannot be valid in both directions for these components. Construction and verification of each pair: Appendix A.5 and C1 of B.4. ∎  
(2) Negative determination is impossible. For any class-F instance and any declared T (every twin below keeps C\_op unchanged):  
　r: add a same-beat pair consisting of an out-of-boundary constant law +a and an in-boundary constant law −a (a \> ε) acting on one position in T; Prof is unchanged while Src = 1. Hence Y₁ alone does not return r = 0.  
　Part, HDyn: if at some beat within W some y ∈ T has s\_t(y) \> ε, a twin with unchanged Prof can be constructed: (i) if y has no prior formation record, first add a pulse +a acting on y under the input u₀ of the entry beat, offset by an in-boundary constant law −a that runs only under u₀; (ii) then add an in-boundary pure-read process reading y, giving Part = 1; or add a pair +k·s(y), −k·s(y) (\|k·s\_t(y)\| \> ε) acting on one position in T, giving HDyn = 1. Hence in this case Y₁ alone returns neither Part = 0 nor HDyn = 0.  
　Conversely, if all positions of T stay ≤ ε throughout W, then Avail is empty in every twin, Part = HDyn = 0, and 0 can be returned.  
　Nor does Part return 1 without a carrier declaration: moving all carriers outside the boundary gives a twin with unchanged Prof and Part = 0.  
Finite verification: C6 of Appendix B.4. ∎  
(3) The criterion for q. When s(x) ≤ ε at window end, q(x) = 0, using only Y₁. Under a class-F declaration and REC there is in addition: when s(x) \> ε at window end and s(x) ≤ ε at some beat in the record, q(x) = 1; when s(x) \> ε at window end and s(x) \> ε throughout the record, q(x) = ⊥. Reading threshold crossing as formation relies on the booking rule of class F; without a class-F declaration, subthreshold changes can accumulate beat by beat and cross the threshold without any single effective change, so q = 1 is not among the verdicts available to Y₁ alone.  
Proof: The first clause follows from Ret. Second clause: the net increment at the beat of threshold crossing is positive; in F only effective components and effective pulses are booked (Mathematical Form 4.2), so there is a positive effective component c \> ε, and s ≥ 0, whence s + c \> ε, which enters Form. Third clause: the formation pair of (1) gives both values. ∎

### 5.3  Mechanism-Level Identifiability

Theorem 6 (mechanism-level identifiability)  
Under SEP, MOD, LOC and BEAT, for every beat t of the actual history and pre-beat state s\_t:  
(1) The knockout difference is the threshold-truncated booked term: Δ\_m(t, s\_t) = ĉ\_m(s\_t). Hence Act\_F(m) = \[\|Δ\_m\| \> ε\], in both directions. Likewise for pulses.  
(2) Let i be an available position, D\_i(s\_t) nonempty, and δ ∈ D\_i(s\_t) an applied legitimate perturbation.  
　(A) If m is effective (\|c\_m(s\_t)\| \> ε), then R\_m(i; δ) = \[i ∈ Supp\_act(m, s\_t)\] for every legitimate δ, in both directions.  
　(B) If m is ineffective, then R\_m(i; δ) = 1 ⇒ i ∈ Supp\_act(m, s\_t); R\_m(i; δ) = 0 does not imply i ∉ Supp\_act.  
(3) If m declares OUT-f, then for an available position i, \[Out\_m(t, s\_t + δe\_i) ≠ Out\_m(t, s\_t)\] = \[i ∈ Rd(m)\], in both directions. For an ineffective process with no declared OUT-f, read dependence returns 1 only when (2)(B) gives a positive response, and is otherwise ⊥\_Sub.  
(4) Reconstruction has two levels.  
　(A) Event-by-event reconstruction: for every event in the inventory and every beat, the booked term ĉ\_m, Act\_F, the functional support and evidence edges of effective processes on certified available positions, and the reads of OUT-f processes are given exactly by (1)–(3), in both directions. This needs only SEP, MOD, LOC, BEAT and INV-a (and OUT-f for OUT-f processes).  
　(B) Full-history reconstruction: Form, Mark, Avail, Trace\_F, Part\_F and HDyn\_F are existential or universal compounds over all events and all beats. Their value 1 needs only one witness certified by (A) and no completeness antecedent. Their value 0 additionally needs INV-c (no events outside the inventory), REC (the record covers t₀ − 1 to t₁) and ENT (the formative history before the record start is registered), and, for Trace\_F, also POS. If any antecedent is missing, ⊥ (⊥\_Inv, ⊥\_Rec or ⊥\_Id) stands where 0 would have stood. Part\_F is in addition subject to the information order: when only processes that are both ineffective and without OUT-f remain, it is ⊥\_Sub.  
Proof:  
(1) By SEP, Φ\_t(s) − s is the sum of the effective pulses and the effective components; by MOD, Φ\_t^{−m}(s) − s lacks exactly the booked term ĉ\_m(s) of m.  
(2) We first show that the finite difference agrees with the support of Mathematical Form D15. Write f(δ) := c\_m(s + δe\_i) − c\_m(s); i is available, so s(i) \> ε ≥ 0.  
　Constant law: f ≡ 0; Supp\_act = ∅.  
　Linear law: f(δ) = n\_i·k·δ, where n\_i is the number of occurrences of i in R(m); for δ ≠ 0, f ≠ 0 ⇔ k ≠ 0 ∧ i ∈ R(m) ⇔ i ∈ Supp\_act.  
　Distinct-position bilinear law, R(m) = (j₁, j₂), i = j₁: f(δ) = k·δ·s(j₂); f ≠ 0 ⇔ k ≠ 0 ∧ s(j₂) ≠ 0 ⇔ j₁ ∈ Supp\_act. The case i = j₂ is symmetric; for i ∉ R(m), f ≡ 0.  
　Same-position bilinear law, R(m) = (i, i): f(δ) = k·δ·(2s(i) + δ). A legitimate δ satisfies s(i) + δ ≥ 0, so 2s(i) + δ ≥ s(i) \> 0; f ≠ 0 ⇔ k ≠ 0 ⇔ i ∈ Supp\_act (since s(i) ≠ 0). At s(i) = 0, f(δ) = kδ² ≠ 0 while the partial derivative is zero; that point is not an available position, and the criterion does not apply.  
　Next we show that threshold truncation produces neither false positives nor misses. Write c₀ = c\_m(s), c₁ = c\_m(s + δe\_i).  
　(A) \|c₀\| \> ε: ĉ(s) = c₀ ≠ 0. If c₁ = c₀, ĉ is the same and the response is 0. If c₁ ≠ c₀: when \|c₁\| \> ε, ĉ(s + δe\_i) = c₁ ≠ c₀; when \|c₁\| ≤ ε, ĉ(s + δe\_i) = 0 ≠ c₀; the response is 1. Hence R\_m(i; δ) = \[f(δ) ≠ 0\] = \[i ∈ Supp\_act\].  
　(B) \|c₀\| ≤ ε: ĉ(s) = 0. Response 1 ⇒ \|c₁\| \> ε ≥ \|c₀\| ⇒ c₁ ≠ c₀ ⇒ f ≠ 0 ⇒ i ∈ Supp\_act. Conversely, when c₁ ≠ c₀ but \|c₁\| ≤ ε the response is 0, so absence of a response does not imply absence of support. Borderline example: linear law x/2, ε = 1/2, s(x) = 1, c₀ = ε (ineffective); δ = 1 crosses the threshold and the response is 1; δ = −1/2 stays below the threshold and the response is 0; in both cases x ∈ Supp\_act.  
(3) By the position-wise injectivity of OUT-f. Take two processes with zero components and the same trajectory, one actually reading x and one not: under a constant read-out their public observations are identical, they cannot be decided in either direction, and ⊥\_Sub is returned; under OUT-f one read-out changes and the other does not, returning 1 and 0.  
(4) (A) follows from (1)–(3). (B) follows by substituting Mathematical Form D16–D19 into 4.6 item by item: an existential 1 is given by a single witness; a universal 0 requires that 0 has been returned for all events, all beats and all candidate positions; INV-c guarantees that no event lies outside the inventory, REC and ENT guarantee that no formation or cause lies outside the record, and POS gives cross-time identity. If any antecedent is missing, something remains unseen and 0 cannot be returned. ∎  
Finite verification: C7 (local response and functional support) and C2 (reconstruction checked against the F-layer evaluator) of Appendix B.4; the readings are listed in the verification index of Appendix B.  
Evidential strength of the verification: the reference truth of the appendix is generated from the known model structure—read positions, read certifications and evidence edges are set to 1 one by one according to the model's read positions (to\_spec of Appendix A.4)—and is the F encoding of that known model. The verifications above show that the Y₂ inversion reproduces this F encoding under the listed protocols. They are not read and route certifications obtained from independent material observation; conclusions at the material layer further depend on the real antecedents of Theorem 7. The reference truth, the Y₂ inversion verdicts and the material-layer read-back qualification are kept apart.  
Perturbation coverage: (2) presupposes that D\_i(s\_t) is nonempty and that a perturbation has been applied. When, for some candidate position, D\_i(s\_t) is empty, no perturbation has been applied, or the applied candidate is not legitimate, (2) gives no conclusion for that position, and the negative determinations that depend on it fail (⊥\_Pert, ⊥\_Obs of 4.6(2)). For a one-sided setting domain a single legitimate nonzero perturbation suffices; (A) holds for every legitimate δ (C14 of Appendix B.4).  
Lemma 6.1 (the load-bearing character of the antecedent lists of negative determinations)  
Fix the observation facts (trajectories, knockout differences, local responses, read-outs), the candidate positions and event sets, and the evaluation tree of the verdict. Removing a declared antecedent only downgrades the qualification of the atoms that depend on it to undetermined; observations are not reselected and quantifier domains are not shrunk. Under this premise, for every 0 verdict obtained by 4.6 (Form, Mark, Act, Cond, HistCond, Trace, Part, HDyn, Q) and its antecedent list B: removing any antecedent in B makes the verdict no longer 0.  
Load-bearing here means logical load-bearing: once the antecedent is removed, the original proof path fails. This differs from program-execution antecedents: once one of those is removed, the observation procedure may no longer have legitimate inputs (for instance, without LOC no local setting can be carried out). Program-execution antecedents are checked by the declaration entry of 4.2; invalid ones return ⊥\_Dom and do not enter the evaluation tree of this lemma.  
Proof: By structural induction on the composition of the verdict. Atoms: a 0 for Form or Mark requires every item in its list to be declared, and if any is missing, ⊥ is returned by 4.6(6)(7); a 0 for Act and a 0 for HistCond or Cond all presuppose PROTO, without which no Y₂ adjudication is made; the formation-negation antecedents merged into a 0 for HistCond or Cond belong to excluded positions that have a response or have not been perturbed, and removing any one of them turns that position into a position of undetermined availability, so ⊥ is returned by 4.6(2)(3); when OUT-f is removed, Cond of an ineffective process returns ⊥\_Sub. Disjunction: the list is the union of the disjuncts' lists; the removed antecedent belongs to some disjunct, which becomes non-0, so the disjunction is no longer 0; when the INV-c merged into Part or HDyn is removed, ⊥ is returned by 4.6(4)(5). Conjunction: the list is the intersection of the lists of the 0 conjuncts; the removed antecedent belongs to every 0 conjunct, all of which become non-0, so the conjunction is no longer 0. ∎  
Finite verification: C15 of Appendix B.4; the readings are listed in the verification index of Appendix B.  
Lemma 8 (closure check)  
Under SEP, MOD and INV-a: (1) INV-c ⇒ closure residual ρ\_t ≡ 0; (2) ρ\_t ≠ 0 ⇒ the inventory is incomplete or the host is not separable; (3) ρ\_t ≡ 0 does not imply that the inventory is complete.  
Proof: (1) and (2) follow from SEP and Theorem 6(1). (3) Counterexample: hide a cancelling pair of processes +x/2, −x/2 from the inventory; the residual is zero, while HDyn is misjudged as 0 (C3). ∎  
Hence INV-c is partly testable, but a zero residual cannot establish it.

### 5.4  Adjudication of Fld

Lemma 9 (reduction to the maximal Trace-Response set; model layer)  
Premises: (A) One run of F is fixed—Structural Positions, pulses and their occurrence certifications, mechanisms with their numerical laws and action certifications, and the edge set and closure convention of the evidence-path graph; every Q\_F(T) is evaluated on this same run, so the numerical trajectory, Form, Mark, Supp and the evidence graph do not depend on T, and in Mathematical Form D16–D19 only Avail and \[tgt ∈ T\] vary with T. (B) Ident and HistEv are the same atomic values, independent of T.  
Conclusion: in the truth order, max\_{∅ ≠ T ⊆ I} Q\_F(T) = Q\_F(T\_max); when T\_max = ∅, Q\_F is 0 for every T.  
Proof:  
1\. For T ⊄ T\_max, the conjunction of Ret and Form is 0.  
2\. For T ⊆ T\_max, by (A), the following are monotonically non-decreasing in T in the truth order: Src (∃-type); Avail\_cond ∩ T and Avail\_act ∩ T; \[tgt ∈ T\]; Path(source set, m)—the sets reachable through 1-edges only and through non-0 edges only both grow as the source set expands (Mathematical Form D14).  
3\. By (B), Ident and HistEv take the same value for every T.  
4\. The strong Kleene operations are monotone in the truth order. ∎  
This lemma is only a reduction of the existential quantifier inside the F model. Finite verification: C4 of Appendix B.4.  
Theorem 7 (material-layer read-back of the Fld verdict)  
(1) Positive direction: suppose the antecedents of a 1 verdict listed in 4.7 hold, IMP holds and Q\_F(T) = 1 (for any T, in particular T\_max). Then by Theorem 5 of the Mathematical Form (of the 8.2 antecedents, (1)–(4) and (6) are borne by the atomic rules of 4.6 and the listed protocols, and (5) by the functional support of 4.6(2) together with IMP), T satisfies the three duties on χ; by Corollary 1 of the General Form, Fld(χ).  
(2) Negative direction: Q\_F(T\_max) = 0 ⇒ ¬Fld(χ) requires the following encoding-fidelity conditions to hold for all T ⊆ Ξ\_B:  
　EF1  Position-inventory completeness: every Structural Position on χ that may bear Trace-Responses is in the position set I of F.  
　EF2  Process-inventory completeness: INV-c, and the host has no indecomposable joint processes (SEP).  
　EF3  Consistency of effectiveness: BEAT.  
　EF4  Encoding completeness of conditioning relations: every Cond and HistCond relation on χ appears as same-beat functional support or as an OUT-f read—no occurrence, timing or location decided by Trace-Responses (Mathematical Form 8.2(6), D18); no multi-beat transmission through intermediate nodes outside T; no zero-component process that actually reads without a declared OUT-f.  
　EF5  Identity and record: POS, REC and ENT hold for all T.  
Proof: Under EF1–EF5 and the antecedents of a 1 verdict, every T that satisfies the three duties on χ lies within I (EF1), and every fact bearing its duties has a corresponding atomic value 1 in F (EF2–EF5 and 4.6); so Q\_F(T) = 1, and by Lemma 9, Q\_F(T\_max) = 1. Contrapositive: Q\_F(T\_max) = 0 ⇒ no T satisfies the three duties ⇒ ¬Fld(χ), by the contrapositive of Theorem 1 of the General Form. ∎  
An example where EF4 fails: a process acting on x has constant strength 1/2 but occurs only when s(y) \> 1/2. The F encoding records only “occurs, constant law 1/2”, so Q\_F(T\_max) = 0; yet HistCond in the sense of the General Form holds (occurrence is determined in part by the Trace-Response y), and HDyn holds on the host. Here setting y to 0 under LOC gives response 1: the intervention evidence shows dependence while the F encoding has no support, the host does not fall under the class-F declaration, and the negative determination fails (C10).  
(3) Mathematical Form 8.4: Q = 0 on a declared T does not imply that no other T satisfies the three duties. This theorem covers every T within I by Lemma 9, and those outside I by EF1.  
5.5  Conditional Bridges above the First Layer  
Lemma 10 (the Dep₀ bridge under SAM)  
Under class F, a beat t ∈ W, x ∈ T, y an available position, and SAM (the events acting on x at that beat are exactly the process m, and no pulse acts on x):  
　\[Dep₀(x, y; s\_t(y), s\_t(y) + δ) ∧ \|Φ\_t(s\_t)(x) − s\_t(x)\| \> ε\] ⇔ \[Act\_F(m) ∧ y ∈ Supp\_act(m, s\_t)\], where δ ∈ D\_y(s\_t) is an applied legitimate perturbation; when D\_y(s\_t) is empty or no perturbation has been applied, this lemma gives no verdict, and ⊥ is returned.  
When the setting of y satisfies LOC (including exclusivity), the right-hand side, together with the evidence-edge certification of 4.6(2), is a witness of HDyn.  
Proof: Under SAM the net increment is the booked term of m; the second conjunct on the left says that m is effective; when m is effective, observed dependence is equivalent to true dependence, as in Theorem 6(2)(A). ∎  
Finite verification: C5 of Appendix B.4. Counterexamples once SAM is removed: cancelling dependence—+y/2 and 1/2 − y/2 act on x, Dep₀ = 0 while HDyn = 1; subthreshold dependence—ε = 1/2, constant law 1 and y/4, Dep₀ = 1 with an effective net change, while HDyn = 0.  
Local net dependence does not suffice to decide HDyn: a constant law 1 and y − 1 act on x, with actual y = 1. The two-sided derivative of the net increment with respect to y is 1 and continuous, yet the dependent mechanism takes the value zero at the actual state and exerts no action, so HDyn = 0 (C5).  
Lemma 11 (conditional bridge for Part)  
Under AC, LOCZ, a probe actually occurring within W, and act a deterministic read-out: if act(s\_t^act) ≠ act(s\_t^ref), then Cond(π\_act, Org(T)), and hence Part. In the stochastic case, take TV \> r.  
Proof: π\_act is a pure-read process whose output depends only on its read positions; the two states differ only on available positions of T and the outputs differ, so π\_act reads one of these available positions; by Mathematical Form D15 and D18, Cond\_F = 1; by Section 3 of the General Form, this is Cond. ∎  
Lemma 12 (Hist → r under PE)  
Under PE, REC and BEAT, if the signal entry positions within segment σ all belong to T and Hist\_W(σ) = 1, then r(T).  
Proof: Under PE, signals enter only as pulses on entry positions. If no pulse in σ is effective, none is booked (Mathematical Form 4.2), so the actual history and the reference history coincide beat by beat and Hist\_W = 0. Hence σ contains an effective pulse; its entry position belongs to T, and Mark ≠ ∅. ∎  
Finite verification: C5 of Appendix B.4. Counterexample once PE is removed: the gated side of the trace-leaving pair of Theorem 5(1), with Hist\_W = 1 while r fails. Under PE, if a complete pulse record with pulse amounts is available, r is read directly from the record; Hist is useful in measurements where pulse amounts cannot be obtained.  
Lemma 13 (a one-way criterion for in-boundary routes)  
Let the carrier of ρ lie inside B, and let Act and HistCond have been certified by 4.6 (via available position i). If R\_ρ(i) = 1 under external-clamp LOC, then there is a material route lying entirely inside B through which the Trace-Responses of T take part in determining the action of ρ, and ρ is a witness of the in-boundary self-feedback type of Section 7 of the General Form.  
Proof: Clamping makes every route through out-of-boundary components or processes with out-of-boundary carriers take baseline values, independent of the perturbation; any dependence that remains can only be transmitted through in-boundary components. ∎  
The converse fails: dependence may vanish under the external clamp because of clamped mixed states (E18), or because in-boundary and out-of-boundary routes cancel; hence the absence of a response under external-clamp LOC does not return 0. The reading used: “the route through which HistCond passes lies inside B” is taken as “there exists a route of participation in determination that lies entirely inside B”.

### 5.6  Protection of the Undetermined

Theorem 8 (protection of the undetermined) (i) From Det\_X = ⊥ one may infer neither X nor ¬X. (ii) There are instances in which X holds while Det\_X = ⊥ under Y₁.  
Proof: (i) By the definition of verdicts: every reason code of ⊥ records only the state of the adjudication method or of the evidence, and none of 4.3–4.8 makes X or ¬X a condition for outputting ⊥. (ii) The side with duty value 1 in each pair of Theorem 5(1). ∎

6\. Declaration of the Bounds of Misjudgment  
The structural adjudication domain of model mode introduces no statistical misjudgment rate: the adjudication discipline is to fall back to ⊥ whenever correctness cannot be guaranteed. A 1 verdict in measurement mode remains subject to the α and multiple-comparison control of 4.3.  
Negative-determination domain: in model mode, a first-layer 0 is correct within Dom relative to the declared k, H\_max and 𝓟; a 0 for Tr in a finite deterministic model is correct for all legitimate finite histories when the full-graph search has been completed or H\_max is not less than the bound of Theorem 1 (Theorem 1), and is otherwise an in-range 0; a second-layer 0 is correct under the antecedents listed in 4.6 and 4.7 (Theorems 6 and 7). When reading back to a material instance, a 1 verdict further requires IMP and Dom4, and a 0 verdict further requires EF1–EF5 (Theorem 7). Measurement mode does not return 0.  
Positive-determination domain: a first-layer 1 is correct within Dom (Theorem 3). A second-layer 1 is correct under the antecedents of a 1 verdict listed in 4.7 (Theorem 7(1)); a 1 under Y₁ plus structural declarations is correct under the antecedents of Lemmas 10–12.  
Where both cannot be secured:  
E1  Slow transients judged as retention differences: reduces to the choice of q (Lemma 5); Tr^cut excludes supply-maintained forms but not slow leakage.  
E2  Shallow traces retained for less than q: first-layer predicates are relative to q; Tr = 0 does not imply the absence of Trace-Responses.  
E3  Multi-position accumulation: a 0 verdict states k; under clamped comparison k cannot be derived from multi-position witnesses (Theorem 2(b)); measurement mode does not return 0.  
E4  Conservation, redundancy, correlated states: Lemma 1 (joint distribution).  
E5  Statistical misjudgment: measurement mode, 4.3.  
E6  Unfaithful model: Dom4.  
E7  Incomplete inventory: the conditional formulation of Dom5; at the second layer, borne by INV-c and EF1, EF2.  
E8  Probes that write: Dom3; the “no write detected” of Act\_𝓟 (b) is relative to window and threshold.  
E9  Partition between supply and signal: the external supply schedule must be independent of the signal history; the actual energy exchange may vary with state.  
E10  Level: when spatial states are merged, the block partition is preserved, the time step is unchanged and the coarse-grained law is an exact quotient, coarse-graining can turn Tr from 1 into 0 but not from 0 into 1; this fails under temporal coarse-graining, approximate coarse-grained laws and changes of boundary.  
E11  Supply cut-off erasing real Trace-Responses: Lemma 6; Tr^cut = 0 does not imply the absence of Trace-Responses.  
E12  Upper bound on history length too small: for finite deterministic models take the bound of Theorem 1; otherwise a 0 is relative to H\_max.  
E13  Choice of starting point: Dom6.  
E14  Spontaneous activity: clause (b) of Act\_𝓟.  
E15  Read-out that writes, combined with spontaneous activity: both (a) and (b) may fail; Act\_𝓟 = 0 means only that there is no witness within the protocol class, not that Trace-Responses did not take part in the activity.  
E16  Redundant formation routes: Lemma 7.  
E17  Witness direction: under clamping a witness is an ordered pair; that (h, h′) is a witness does not imply that (h′, h) is.  
E18  Mixed-state witnesses: clamping can bring the control side into states the original dynamics cannot reach and so produce witnesses; such witnesses only make the first-layer predicate 1 and do not count as evidence of an action chain.  
E19  Twins under state-level observation: Theorem 5; the corresponding duty returns ⊥\_Prof.  
E20  Knockout-induced compensation (MOD fails): knocking out either of two cancelling processes induces compensation by the other, the knockout difference is zero, HDyn is misjudged as 0, and the closure residual is zero and raises no alarm (verification C3).  
E21  Hidden cancelling processes (INV-c fails): the residual is zero while HDyn is misjudged as 0 (Lemma 8); a hidden non-cancelling process makes the residual nonzero and can be detected.  
E22  Side effects of setting (LOC exclusivity fails): setting x also changes y, a process reading y is mistaken for one depending on x, a spurious evidence edge arises, and HDyn is misjudged as 1 (verification C3, C9).  
E23  Read dependence of ineffective processes: invisible under threshold truncation; without OUT-f, ⊥\_Sub (Theorem 6(2)(B), (3)).  
E24  Read-out decoupled from reading: constant or lagged outputs do not satisfy OUT-f; Part via a pure read returns ⊥\_Sub (verification C8).  
E25  Unfaithful encoding: occurrence decided by Trace-Responses and the like; the F encoding gives Q = 0 while HDyn holds on the host; a negative determination requires EF1–EF5 (Theorem 7(2), verification C10).  
E26  Indecomposable joint net zero: ⊥\_Joint.  
E27  Dependence vanishing under the external clamp: mixed states or two cancelling routes; 0 is not returned (Lemma 13); a 0 for the in-boundary self-feedback type is given only by the universal condition of 4.8.  
E28  False structural declarations: when PE, SAM, AC or LOCZ fails, the 1 verdicts of Lemmas 10–12 can be wrong; counterexamples with each declaration removed are given in 5.5.  
E29  Unregistered formative history before the starting point: without a declared ENT, Form and Mark of positions already above ε at the record start return ⊥\_Rec (verification C11).  
E30  No certified doubly true process does not mean no candidate: when a process in B has Act = 1 and HistCond = ⊥, the in-boundary self-feedback type returns ⊥ (verification C13).  
E31  Insufficient perturbation coverage: when a candidate position has no legitimate nonzero perturbation, no perturbation has been applied, or the applied candidate is not legitimate, the negative determinations that depend on its local response return ⊥\_Pert or ⊥\_Obs (verification C14).  
E32  Conflating coupling and marginals: the marginal TV of a history compared with itself depends on the coupling only when there is overwriting; marginal TV and disagreement probability are kept apart (3.3, Appendix B.1).  
E33  Parameters outside the declared domain: r ∉ \[0, 1) or negative r\_act, r\_μ, r\_ρ return ⊥\_Dom (Dom2, Appendix B.1); invalid transition kernels or disturbance distributions likewise return ⊥\_Dom (3.3).  
E34  Cross-beat correlated disturbances not declared as augmented states: verdicts computed under beat-wise independence do not extend to the instance; with the same single-beat marginal, cross-beat independence and cross-beat correlation can give different TV values (3.3, Appendix B.1).  
The real adjudication boundary is the decidable domain together with the declared conditions, not a statistical confidence interval.

7\. Adjudication Theorem

### 7.1  Adjudication Theorem

Theorem 9 (adjudication theorem) Under Dom1–Dom6 and the declared instance protocols:  
(i) in model mode, provided the declaration entry of 4.2 has been passed, the 1 and 0 of each first-layer verdict are correct (Theorem 3); invalid declarations return ⊥\_Dom, which is not read as 0; a 0 for Act\_𝓟 is relative to the declared 𝓟, and a 0 for Dep to a completely enumerated finite V\_y; a 0 for Tr counts as a whole-history negative determination only if the full-graph search has been completed or H\_max is not less than the bound of Theorem 1, and is otherwise only an in-range 0 (4.3); a 1 in measurement mode is as in 4.3;  
(ii) with Y₁ alone (C\_op readable, mechanism inventory not readable), the second-layer verdicts take values as in 4.5, of which the 0 for q, and the 0 for Part and HDyn when T stays ≤ ε throughout W, are correct; the rest are ⊥\_Prof and cannot be removed in both directions by any first-layer criterion (Theorem 5);  
(iii) with Y₁ plus the declarations Y\_decl, the 1 for q under a class-F declaration, the degenerate negative determination of Fld under position-inventory completeness (Dom5, process inventory not included), and the 1 verdicts of Lemmas 10–12 are correct;  
(iv) under Y₂, the atoms of 4.6 are correct in their 1 and 0 verdicts under their listed antecedents (a 0 requires perturbation coverage and the inventory and record antecedents, otherwise ⊥; two-way adjudication holds only on the subdomain where the antecedents are satisfied item by item); the Fld verdict of 4.7, as an F-model verdict, is correct in its 1 under the antecedents of a 1 verdict, and its 0 further requires EF1–EF5; for Fld read back to a material instance, a 1 further requires IMP and Dom4 (Theorems 6 and 7, Lemma 9);  
(v) a 1 verdict of the in-boundary self-feedback type is correct; its 0 is given only under INV-c and EF4, once Act\_F or HistCond\_F of every running process in B has been determined to be 0 (4.8, Lemma 13);  
(vi) from ⊥ one may infer neither direction (Theorem 8).  
The paths and conditions used by each verdict are listed separately as in Section 4 and are not conjoined across verdicts.

### 7.2  Condition Ledger

H\_new = ∅. The theorems of this paper depend on no unproved antecedent. Dom1–Dom6, SEP, MOD, LOC, BEAT, OUT-f, INV-a, INV-c, REC, ENT, POS, PE, SAM, AC, LOCZ, IMP and EF1–EF5 are conditions of the adjudication-method layer; they do not enter the definition of any duty, nor do they propagate wholesale under the name “Adjudication Form”. Misjudgments caused by their failure belong to Section 6; what is breached is the corresponding condition.

### 7.3  Prohibited Read-Backs

A first-layer predicate equal to 1 must not be read as the duty being 1 unless the bridge and protocol used are cited.  
A first-layer predicate equal to 0, Hist = 0 or Act\_𝓟 = 0 must not be read as the duty being 0.  
A zero closure residual must not be read as completeness of the inventory.  
Q = 0 on a declared T must not be read as ¬Fld; that requires Lemma 9 and EF1–EF5.  
Dep must not be read as evidence of HistCond; that role is borne by Dep₀, and only under SAM.  
The absence of a response under external-clamp LOC must not be used to return 0 for the in-boundary self-feedback type; a 0 for that type is given only by the universal condition of 4.8.  
Cross-time identity must not be inferred from numerical equality.  
Model-mode verdicts must not be extrapolated to physical objects without declaring Dom4.  
Finite verification must not be read as a universal conclusion.  
The value of one verdict must not be used to fill the ⊥ of another.

### 7.4  Breach Modes

K1  Within Dom and the declared protocols, there is an instance for which the verdict is 0 while the property holds.  
K2  Within Dom and the declared protocols, there is an instance for which the verdict is 1 while the property does not hold.  
K3  There is a criterion taking only Y₁ and the frozen C\_op as input that decides r, HDyn, Part or Fld correctly in both directions on all valid instances (contradicting Theorem 5).  
K4  Under SEP, MOD, LOC and BEAT, the local response of some effective process disagrees with the D15 functional support (contradicting Theorem 6(2)(A)).  
K5  OUT-f holds while the read-out response disagrees with the actual read positions.  
K6  The T\_max reduction fails under the premises of Lemma 9.  
K7  EF1–EF5 hold and Q\_F(T\_max) = 0, yet Fld(χ) holds.  
K8  R\_ρ(i) = 1 under external-clamp LOC, yet no route of participation in determination lies entirely inside B.  
K9  The 0 conditions of 4.8 hold, yet a witness of the in-boundary self-feedback type exists in B.  
Not breaches: misjudgments caused by failure of adjudication-domain conditions or instance protocols (Section 6), where what is breached is the corresponding condition; the existence of the ⊥ region; duty counterexamples argued from a first-layer verdict of 0.

### 7.5  Representative Instances

(1) First-layer model instances: each is a load-bearing counterexample or a finite verification of a theorem and is cited at the theorem it belongs to (3.3, Theorems 1, 2 and 4, Dom2, Lemmas 2–7); all readings and their sources are listed in verification index 1 of Appendix B, and the run results are in Appendix B.1, B.2 and B.3.  
(2) Second-layer verifications: C1–C17 verify, in order, Theorems 5–7, Lemma 6.1, Lemmas 8–12, the declaration entry of 4.2, 4.6, 4.8 and the report of 4.9, each cited at the theorem it belongs to; the readings are listed in verification index 2 of Appendix B, and the run results are in Appendix B.4. The second-layer verifications take the F encoding of the known model as reference truth; they are not material certification (see the note on evidential strength after Theorem 6).  
(3) Host Instances  
Host instances are chosen according to the criteria this Form is to test: a chosen instance must be able to test two-way validity, bounds of Misjudgment or observation protocols. Each instance states the declared χ, T, observation level, protocols, mechanism evidence, verdicts and read-back antecedents. Mathematical construction, computational simulation, literature evidence and material experiment are kept apart and do not substitute for one another.  
Instance A  Minimal representative model of the MLP candidate (computational simulation, model mode; Appendix A.7, B.5)  
Declaration: P is the program of a single-weight linear unit; L is the algorithmic level, with state variable w and ε = 1/100; the entry beat is 0 and W is beats 1–3. Signals: an out-of-boundary initialization pulse w ← w + 1 at the entry beat; training input a (one sample, x = 1, target 2). Processes: the forward read-out fwd, a pure-read process inside B with faithful read-out; the optimizer opt, which under input a gives Δw = η(2 − w), η = 1/4, a linear law. T = {w}. Three adjudication instances: χ\_ext, with the carrier of opt outside the boundary and B consisting of w and fwd; χ\_in, with B extended to include opt, which is a separate adjudication instance; χ\_frz, the frozen deployment of χ\_ext, whose input within W is rest.  
Observation level and protocols: at the algorithmic level the program is the host, and the knockouts, local settings and read-outs of Y₂ can all be carried out exactly. The conditions are listed in three classes by source (H0 of Appendix B.5). The Boolean declarations of full\_decl in the appendix program only hand the conditions to the adjudicator and prove nothing; each item below is borne separately:  
　Held by the model by definition: IMP (the numerical law reads the set state variable w directly), BEAT (discrete beats), POS (the position is the state variable), REC (the record covers every beat), INV-a and INV-c (the inventory is all the processes in the program; a closure check without residual is only a necessary condition, Lemma 8), EF1–EF3 and EF5 (the position set is {w}, and the level is the program itself), and EF4 (whether a process runs depends only on the beat and the input, components depend on the read positions only through class-F numerical laws, and no occurrence, timing or location is decided by Trace-Responses).  
　Obtained by actually executing interventions or computations on the simulation bench: SEP and MOD (each process is knocked out beat by beat along the training and frozen trajectories; the net change is exactly the sum of the effective components, and a knockout removes exactly that component); LOC and its exclusivity (local setting of w changes only w, and the evidence edges are obtained from actual settings); OUT-f (the read-out of fwd equals the value of w, and setting w changes the read-out); ENT (w = 0 ≤ ε at the record start).  
　Conditions assumed by Boolean flags alone, without such bearing: none.  
These are all conditions of this model, not certifications of any material host.  
Verdicts:  
χ\_ext: the trajectory of w is 0, 1, 5/4, 23/16, 101/64. Under Y₂, Act(opt) = 1; local setting of w changes the booked term of opt, the evidence edge (w, t) → opt is certified 1, and HistCond = 1; fwd reads out according to w, so Part = 1; the initialization pulse leaves a trace, so r = 1; Fld = 1 (Theorem 7(1)). The only running process in B is fwd, whose Act\_F = 0; under INV-c and EF4, the in-boundary self-feedback type returns 0 (4.8); without a declared INV-c it returns ⊥. This 0 is a verdict on the complete model at the algorithmic level: EF4 holds at that level by the program text, and it does not extend to a negative determination on any material realization. With Y₁ alone, each duty returns ⊥\_Prof; adding the class-F declaration, w crosses the threshold within the record, so q = 1.  
χ\_in: Fld = 1; opt in B has Act = HistCond = 1 at once; there are no out-of-boundary components, the response under external-clamp LOC is unchanged, and the in-boundary self-feedback type returns 1. With Y₁ plus SAM and LOC, Lemma 10 returns HDyn = 1.  
χ\_frz: Part = 1; no process acts on w within W, so HDyn = 0; under INV-c and EF1–EF5, Fld = 0 (Theorem 7(2)). With Y₁ alone, w is above ε within W, and HDyn and Fld return ⊥\_Prof; if the inventory is not declared complete, HDyn returns ⊥\_Inv.  
Criteria tested: changing the declared boundary gives a different adjudication instance, and χ\_ext and χ\_in receive different in-boundary self-feedback verdicts; the frozen deployment is judged 0 under Y₂, validly in both directions, but cannot be judged 0 under Y₁ (Theorem 5(2)); the conditional positive determination of Lemma 10.  
Limits of qualification: this instance is a computational simulation at the algorithmic level; its conclusions belong to the declared minimal model and do not extend to the training procedure or hardware implementation of any concrete MLP system, or to any material host.  
Instance B  *Physarum polycephalum* (material host, measurement mode; verdicts currently undetermined)  
Declaration: P is the tube network of a *Physarum polycephalum* plasmodium; L is tube-segment diameter and cytoplasmic flow, with ε the measurement resolution of diameter; B is the tube network, and the carriers of tube-wall growth and retraction and of peristaltic contraction all lie inside B; W is the experimental time window; T is the set of tube-segment diameters; the signals are nutrient sources or light stimuli at the declared entry points.  
Available observations: time-lapse imaging gives trajectories of tube diameter and flow; replicate plasmodia or pieces divided from the same strain give paired signal histories; local tube cutting, local cooling or pharmacological inhibition of contraction can serve as interventions.  
Verdicts and missing conditions:  
Tr^rest: measurement mode requires the null hypothesis, test method, error control and lower confidence bound to be declared as in 4.3; no data have been declared, so it currently returns ⊥\_Obs.  
q: the retention of tube diameter at window end is read from imaging and belongs to Y₁; the tube diameters were formed before the record start, so their formative history must be registered under ENT, otherwise formation returns ⊥\_Rec; no data have been declared, so it currently returns ⊥\_Obs.  
r, Part, HDyn, Fld: the available interventions do not satisfy MOD (cutting a tube reroutes the flow and induces compensation in other segments), LOC (local cooling changes contraction and flow at the same time and is not exclusive) or INV-c (the processes of tube-wall growth, retraction and peristalsis have not yet been individuated); the feedback law between tube diameter and flow is not one of the three numerical laws of class F, so Theorem 6 does not apply (U9). Hence only Y₁ can be used, and these return ⊥\_Prof; moreover, measurement mode does not return 0.  
Literature leads (not verification under this Form):  
　Kramar, M., Alim, K. Encoding memory in tube diameter hierarchy of living flow network. Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences of the USA, 118(10), e2007815118, 2021. doi:10.1073/pnas.2007815118. Reports that past feeding events leave an imprint in the tube-diameter hierarchy of the plasmodium and, through that hierarchy, influence its subsequent direction of migration.  
　Tero, A., Takagi, S., Saigusa, T., Ito, K., Bebber, D. P., Fricker, M. D., Yumiki, K., Kobayashi, R., Nakagaki, T. Rules for biologically inspired adaptive network design. Science, 327(5964), 439–442, 2010, doi:10.1126/science.1177894. Gives a network model in which tube diameter adapts to flow.  
Both are public literature leads, but neither supplies measurement data obtained under the protocols of this Form. The learning and adaptive properties reported in the literature are not taken directly as mechanism duties at Y₂; they guide the choice of interventions and models when Y₂ evidence is sought, and do not replace material experiments.  
Conditions that gathering evidence under this Form requires and that the available interventions cannot meet: MOD (knocking out individual tube segments without inducing compensation in other segments); LOC exclusivity (changing the diameter of a single segment without simultaneously changing flow and contraction); INV-c (individuating tube-wall growth, retraction and peristalsis separately). Required observation measures: time series of diameter and flow for each segment (resolution ε); stimulus histories of paired plasmodia at the declared entry points; diameter retention within the rest window q after the stimulus is removed; a TV estimate for Tr^rest with its one-sided lower confidence bound.  
When evidence is gathered from public raw data, the null hypothesis, test method, error control and effect size are declared separately under measurement mode as in 4.3, and the limitations of physical experiments are retained; no such data exist at present.  
Criteria tested: measurement mode returns only 1 or ⊥; when instance protocols are unavailable, duty verdicts stay at ⊥ with the missing conditions listed, and neither literature evidence nor model simulation is substituted for them. The only instance on which this Form obtains duty verdicts is Instance A (a computational simulation at the algorithmic level); the verdicts of Instance B are currently ⊥\_Obs or ⊥\_Prof, and the two are not to be cited together as material cases in which duty verdicts have been obtained.

8\. Interfaces  
Upstream inheritance: the main text of the definition in *The Ontological Definition of the Sedimentation-Field*; Sections 3, 5, 7, 8 and 9 of *The General Form of Field Concretization*; D1–D22, 4.2, Theorems 1, 4 and 5, 6.3, 8.2, 8.4, 14.2 and Appendix A.1 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*; Definitions 3, 4 and 9 of *The Mathematical Form of the Field*.  
To *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, 6.3: the destinations of the adjudication predicates among the evidence atoms are registered as in 4.4; the destination of Dep is refined to Dep₀, limited to SAM.  
To *The Indistinguishability Form of Field Concretization*: the profile invariance of the first-layer criteria (Theorem 4) and the witness pairs and twin constructions of Theorem 5 bear only bounds of Misjudgment here. Theorems on general indistinguishability within an observation set, the characterization of profile-equivalence classes and the bounds of observation-set extension belong to that paper; that paper treats at least the following: indistinguishability once MOD or LOC is removed; under which observation sets an indecomposable joint net zero is indistinguishable; the equivalence classes of the choice of reference history for Hist.  
To *The Grading Form of Tendency Types*: that paper takes Tr^rest = 1 as one of its entry conditions, and the first-layer Tr^rest and its qualification boundary are given here as they stand. That paper requires instances to satisfy the three duties and to be of the in-boundary self-feedback type: the duties are adjudicated by 4.6 and 4.7; the in-boundary self-feedback type is adjudicated by 4.8, with positive determinations and conditional negative determinations; a negative determination requires INV-c, EF4 and complete adjudication of the atoms of the processes in B, and the absence of a response under external-clamp LOC does not return 0; the reading used is given in Lemma 13.  
*The Master Plan and Results Archive for the Verification of the 17 Hosts*: evidence records for candidate hosts other than Instances A and B are kept in that archive; the record items for each host are the evidence level used (Y₁, Y\_decl, Y₂), the adjudication mode, the declared instance protocols, the missing conditions and the five-tuple of each verdict; the three kinds of evidence—computational simulation, literature evidence and material experiment—do not substitute for one another.  
Comparison: G-C of *The Adjudication Form of Computation*, the eleventh paper of the Computation Group Form (out-of-boundary solving with write-back returns 0), agrees with this paper on instances χ whose boundary contains only the network.  
Open items (not supporting the conclusions of this paper):  
U1  The bridge between first-layer predicates and duties: open outside class F and the listed protocols.  
U2  The status of retention under supply cut-off.  
U3  The organizational identity condition; the duty of Dep awaits it.  
U4  Negative determination of Fld for measured hosts.  
U5  Out-of-boundary training procedures: which of χ and χ′ is the host in question.  
U6  Activity participation beyond the declared probe class (E15).  
U7  Participation criteria for processes invisible below resolution (⊥\_Sub).  
U8  The action criterion for an indecomposable joint net zero (⊥\_Joint, inherited from upstream).  
U9  Weakest combinations of instance protocols; mechanism-level identifiability beyond class F (nonlinear laws, continuous time); the physical legitimacy of knockout and local setting in measurement.

## Appendix A  Program Source

Runtime environment: Python 3.10 or later, standard library only. Place the seven files in the same directory.  
A.1  layer1.py  First-layer reference adjudicator (exact rational arithmetic, staged supply-cut-off runs), five-tuple report adapter and instance regressions: declaration entry (including per-call override parameters, probes and history segments), checks of transition-kernel validity and supply-cut-off declaration completeness, twin protocol, monitor automaton for the set of legitimate histories, the resolution threshold and its domain, the declared probe class of Act\_𝓟 and the threshold of clause (b), the two thresholds and setting domain of Dep (3.3, 3.4, 4.3, Dom2, Theorems 1 and 2, Lemmas 1–7)

~~~python
"""The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field, Appendix A.1: first-layer reference adjudicator (model mode) and instance regression.
Verdicts: Tr^rest, Tr^cut (exact adjudication by enumeration and by pair automaton), Hist^rest, Hist^cut, Act_𝓟, Act_𝓟^h, Dep.
Readings: 'rest' compares the joint distribution of all in-boundary components; 'cut' rests q steps, then applies q steps of supply cut-off, and compares the joint distribution of the declared block.
Implementation and supported domain of clamping (Section 3.3):
  For a model that declares a structural representation x' = Φ(x, u, ω) and the exogenous disturbance distribution (the struct parameter of Model), the twin protocol maintains the paired joint distribution of the two sides:
  both sides share the same ω; after each transition the control side's out-of-boundary components are overwritten with the baseline side's values; μ_h, μ_h′ are the two marginals of the joint distribution.
  For a model given only by a transition kernel, clamping is restricted to the case in which the baseline side's out-of-boundary components are a point distribution at every step (the overwrite value is then determined, and each side's marginal equals the one the twin protocol gives);
  all other cases yield ⊥_Obs, and the adjudicator does not falsely claim a complete protocol.
Asking for Tr^cut or Hist^cut without a declared supply cut-off yields ⊥_Undef. The valid domain of the resolution threshold r is 0 ≤ r < 1; inputs outside it yield ⊥_Dom and do not enter the formal verdicts.
When the set of legitimate histories 𝓗 is declared by a finite monitor automaton (the mon parameter), enumeration and exact adjudication use only histories accepted on both sides; exact adjudication merges the monitor states into the paired nodes (Theorem 1 (c)).
Clause (b) of Act_𝓟 is a numeric increment predicate: |(act after − act before)₁ − (act after − act before)₂| > r_act, where r_act carries the unit of act and is declared separately; it is adjudicated only when act is a point distribution, otherwise ⊥_Obs.
Exact adjudication (deterministic models) checks TV > r: under point distributions TV ∈ {0, 1}, so it accepts if and only if the compared blocks differ and r < 1;
it returns the number of reachable states N, the number of in-boundary values |I′| on the control side after clamping, and the history-length bound N + N·|I′|·k (Theorem 1).
Dep sets two separate thresholds: r_μ (location effect, in the unit of x) and r_ρ (translation residual, dimensionless).
"""
import itertools
from collections import defaultdict, deque
OBS = '⊥_Obs'; UNDEF = '⊥_Undef'; DOM = '⊥_Dom'
IN0 = '0_in-range'   # on an infinite setting domain, the absence of a Dep witness is established only on the measured level set: in-range 0, not 0 over all of V_y
def _rok(r): return 0 <= r < 1          # Dom2: valid domain of the resolution threshold
class _CutStep:
    """Internal sentinel for the supply cut-off action, kept apart from the namespace of the input alphabet. Every declared input name (including the string '__cut') goes only to the transition law step / phi."""
    __slots__ = ()
    def __repr__(self): return '<supply cut-off>'
CUT = _CutStep()
class BadKernel(Exception):
    """Invalid transition kernel: empty distribution, negative probability, non-finite value, or probabilities not summing to 1. The adjudicator yields ⊥_Dom on it and does not treat it as a valid model."""
from fractions import Fraction
def _q(x):
    """Convert a probability or threshold into an explicit rational: integers and Fractions pass unchanged; a float is taken as the exact rational value of its shortest decimal representation (repr),
    e.g. 0.05 → 1/20. All distributions, TVs, expectations and threshold comparisons in adjudication are carried out exactly over the rationals, with no implicit floating point."""
    if isinstance(x, bool): raise BadKernel('a Boolean is not a probability')
    if isinstance(x, Fraction): return x
    if isinstance(x, int): return Fraction(x)
    if isinstance(x, float):
        if x != x or x in (float('inf'), float('-inf')): raise BadKernel('non-finite value')
        return Fraction(repr(x))
    raise BadKernel('probability is not a number')
def _qn(v):
    """Normalize numeric readings: floats are converted to exact rationals by _q; integers, Fractions and non-numeric values pass unchanged. State tuples are processed component by component."""
    if isinstance(v, tuple): return tuple(_qn(c) for c in v)
    if isinstance(v, float): return _q(v)
    return v
def _prob(d):
    """Validate raw probability masses (duty 1): before any key normalization, merging or push-forward, each probability is converted to a rational;
       each must be nonnegative, and the total must be exactly 1. Keys keep their declared values. Returns {declared key: rational probability} with zero-probability entries removed."""
    if not isinstance(d, dict) or not d: raise BadKernel('empty distribution')
    qd = {k: _q(p) for k, p in d.items()}
    if any(p < 0 for p in qd.values()): raise BadKernel('negative probability')
    if sum(qd.values()) != 1: raise BadKernel('probabilities do not sum to 1')
    return {k: p for k, p in qd.items() if p > 0}
def _kernel(d):
    """State transition kernel (duty 1 + duty 2): first validate the raw probabilities with _prob, then normalize the numeric components of successor states to rationals; successors that coincide after normalization are merged."""
    qd = defaultdict(int)
    for k, p in _prob(d).items(): qd[_qn(k)] += p
    return dict(qd)
def _mon_ok(mon):
    if mon is None: return True
    return (isinstance(mon, tuple) and len(mon) == 3 and isinstance(mon[1], dict) and isinstance(mon[2], (set, frozenset)))
def validate_layer1_decl(M, q=None, k=None, H=None, r=None, thresholds=(), mon=None, reading='rest',
                         init=None, hists=(), seg=None, seg_len=None, idx=()):
    """Single entry point for first-layer declarations (4.2 Dom1–Dom6, 3.1): returns None (pass) or a reason code.
       Model layer: position names, boundary, initial state, alphabet, transition law (M.decl_err); supply cut-off: structural representation lacking cut_phi, or supply cut-off undeclared → ⊥_Undef;
       parameters: q a nonnegative integer, k and H positive integers, r ∈ [0, 1), every threshold nonnegative, monitor automaton well-formed → otherwise ⊥_Dom."""
    if M.decl_err: return DOM
    if reading == 'cut' and M.cut is None: return UNDEF
    if q is not None and not (isinstance(q, int) and q >= 0): return DOM
    for v in (k, H):
        if v is not None and not (isinstance(v, int) and v >= 1): return DOM
    if r is not None and not _rok(r): return DOM
    if any(t is None or t < 0 for t in thresholds): return DOM
    if not _mon_ok(mon): return DOM
    # check the override parameters actually in effect for each call (checking the default initial state at model construction does not replace this)
    if init is not None and not (isinstance(init, tuple) and len(init) == len(M.names)): return DOM
    for h in hists:
        if h is None: continue
        if not isinstance(h, (list, tuple)) or not all(u in M.alpha for u in h): return DOM
    if seg is not None:
        if not isinstance(seg, (list, tuple, set, frozenset)): return DOM
        sl = list(seg)
        if len(set(sl)) != len(sl): return DOM                     # duplicate index
        if not all(isinstance(i, int) and not isinstance(i, bool) and 0 <= i < (seg_len or 0) for i in sl): return DOM
    for i in idx:
        if not (isinstance(i, int) and not isinstance(i, bool) and 0 <= i < len(M.names)): return DOM
    return None
import functools
def _guard(shape=1):
    """Adjudicator entry: an invalid transition kernel yields ⊥_Dom; shape is the length of the returned tuple (1 means scalar)."""
    def deco(f):
        @functools.wraps(f)                      # keep the original signature so the report adapter can bind the parameters actually in effect
        def g(*a, **kw):
            try: return f(*a, **kw)
            except BadKernel: return DOM if shape == 1 else (DOM,) + (None,) * (shape - 1)
        return g
    return deco
def tv(a, b):
    ks = set(a) | set(b); return Fraction(sum(abs(a.get(k, 0) - b.get(k, 0)) for k in ks)) / 2   # exact: a rational divided by 2
def push(d, f):
    m = defaultdict(int)
    for s, p in d.items(): m[f(s)] += p
    return dict(m)
class Model:
    """inside: whether inside the boundary; medium: whether in the declared block compared by Tr^cut; step(s,u)->{s':p};
       cut(s)->{s':p}: one step under supply cut-off (None if undeclared); act(s)->number"""
    def __init__(s, names, medium, inside, step, init, alphabet, act=None, cut=None, struct=None):
        # struct = (phi, omega, cut_phi): phi(s, u, w) -> s', omega = {w: p}, cut_phi(s, w) -> s' (may be None)
        s.struct = struct
        s.decl_err = []                          # declaration-level defects, reported by validate_layer1_decl at the single entry point
        if not names or len(set(names)) != len(names) or not all(isinstance(n, str) and n for n in names):
            s.decl_err.append('position name empty or duplicated')
        if not (len(medium) == len(inside) == len(names)) or (init is not None and len(init) != len(names)):
            s.decl_err.append('lengths of state variables, boundary and initial state differ')
        if not alphabet or len(set(alphabet)) != len(list(alphabet)) or 'rest' not in alphabet:
            s.decl_err.append('alphabet empty, duplicated or missing rest')
        if struct is None and step is None:
            s.decl_err.append('no transition law given')
        s.cut_incomplete = False
        if struct is not None and cut is not None and (len(struct) < 3 or struct[2] is None):
            cut = None; s.cut_incomplete = True  # 3.3: a model in structural representation must declare cut_phi(s, ω) on the same disturbance space; a cut-off kernel alone does not constitute a twin supply cut-off protocol
        s.omega, s.omega_err = None, None
        if struct is not None:
            # exogenous disturbance distribution: raw probabilities validated once at construction (_prob), keys keep their declared values; net transitions, the twin protocol and the cut-off structure all use this same s.omega
            try: s.omega = _prob(struct[1])
            except BadKernel as e:
                s.omega_err = str(e)
                s.decl_err.append('invalid exogenous disturbance distribution: ' + s.omega_err)   # an invalid declaration already detected at construction enters the single entry point; every public adjudication yields ⊥_Dom before evaluation
            phi = struct[0]
            def _om():
                if s.omega is None: raise BadKernel('invalid exogenous disturbance distribution: ' + s.omega_err)
                return s.omega
            step = lambda st, u: push(_om(), lambda w: phi(st, u, w))
            if len(struct) > 2 and struct[2] is not None and cut is None:
                cp = struct[2]; cut = lambda st: push(_om(), lambda w: cp(st, w))
            s._om = _om
        raw_step, raw_cut = step, cut
        alpha_set = set(alphabet)
        def step(st, u):                                          # the transition kernel's normalization is checked at every use; inputs must belong to the alphabet
            if u not in alpha_set: raise BadKernel('input not in the declared alphabet')
            return _kernel(raw_step(st, u))
        if raw_cut is not None: cut = lambda st: _kernel(raw_cut(st))
        # effective state representation: the start is normalized once at construction (float components take their exact decimal value); every run entry starts from this same start
        s.names, s.step, s.init, s.alpha = names, step, (_qn(tuple(init)) if init is not None else None), list(alphabet)
        s.act = act or (lambda st: st)
        s.cut = cut          # supply cut-off undeclared → None; Tr^cut, Hist^cut yield ⊥_Undef
        s.IN = [i for i in range(len(names)) if inside[i]]
        s.EXT = [i for i in range(len(names)) if not inside[i]]
        s.MED = [i for i in s.IN if medium[i]]
    def proj(s, st, idx): return tuple(st[i] for i in idx)
def _adv(d, f):
    n = defaultdict(int)
    for st, p in d.items():
        for x, q in f(st).items(): n[x] += p * q
    return dict(n)
def run_twin(M, h1, h2, init=None, clamp=True, ncut=0, shared=True):
    """Twin protocol: maintains the paired joint distribution {(s1, s2): p}. shared=False means independent sampling on the two sides (for contrast only; not a legitimate protocol)."""
    phi, omega = M.struct[0], M._om()              # the same validated exogenous disturbance distribution as Model.step (keys are the declared values)
    cut_phi = M.struct[2] if len(M.struct) > 2 else None
    s0 = _qn(init if init is not None else M.init)
    J = {(s0, s0): Fraction(1)}
    seq = [(u1, u2) for u1, u2 in zip(h1, h2)] + [(CUT, CUT)] * ncut
    for u1, u2 in seq:
        if u1 is CUT and cut_phi is None: return None
        f = (lambda st, w: cut_phi(st, w)) if u1 is CUT else None
        n = defaultdict(int)
        for (a, b), p in J.items():
            pairs = [(w, w, q) for w, q in omega.items()] if shared else \
                    [(w1, w2, q1 * q2) for w1, q1 in omega.items() for w2, q2 in omega.items()]
            for w1, w2, q in pairs:
                a2 = _qn(f(a, w1) if f else phi(a, u1, w1))
                b2 = _qn(f(b, w2) if f else phi(b, u2, w2))
                if clamp and M.EXT:
                    b2 = tuple(a2[i] if i in M.EXT else v for i, v in enumerate(b2))
                n[(a2, b2)] += p * q
        J = dict(n)
    return push(J, lambda ab: ab[0]), push(J, lambda ab: ab[1])
def twin_joint(M, h1, h2, init=None, clamp=True, shared=True):
    """Returns the paired joint distribution {(s1, s2): p} (without supply cut-off), for computing coupling-event quantities such as Pr(the two sides disagree); kept separate from the TV of the marginals."""
    phi, omega = M.struct[0], M._om()
    s0 = _qn(init if init is not None else M.init)
    J = {(s0, s0): Fraction(1)}
    for u1, u2 in zip(h1, h2):
        n = defaultdict(int)
        for (a, b), p in J.items():
            pairs = [(w, w, q) for w, q in omega.items()] if shared else \
                    [(w1, w2, q1 * q2) for w1, q1 in omega.items() for w2, q2 in omega.items()]
            for w1, w2, q in pairs:
                a2 = _qn(phi(a, u1, w1)); b2 = _qn(phi(b, u2, w2))
                if clamp and M.EXT:
                    b2 = tuple(a2[i] if i in M.EXT else v for i, v in enumerate(b2))
                n[(a2, b2)] += p * q
        J = dict(n)
    return J
def run_pair(M, h1, h2, init=None, clamp=True, ncut=0):
    """Coupled run of two histories, optionally followed by ncut steps of supply cut-off; a CUT sentinel inside the sequence is also a cut-off step (phased run: history → rest → supply cut-off → probe). A model that declares a structural representation goes through the twin protocol (run_twin);
       for a model given only by a transition kernel, clamping requires the out-of-boundary marginal on the h1 side to be a point distribution at every step; otherwise returns None (⊥_Obs)."""
    if M.struct is not None:
        return run_twin(M, h1, h2, init, clamp, ncut)
    s0 = _qn(init if init is not None else M.init)
    d1, d2 = {s0: Fraction(1)}, {s0: Fraction(1)}
    def one(u): return M.cut if u is CUT else (lambda st, u=u: M.step(st, u))   # the sequence may contain the cut-off sentinel CUT
    if any(u is CUT for u in list(h1) + list(h2)) and M.cut is None: return None
    steps = [(one(u1), one(u2)) for u1, u2 in zip(h1, h2)]
    steps += [(M.cut, M.cut)] * ncut
    for f1, f2 in steps:
        d1, d2 = _adv(d1, f1), _adv(d2, f2)
        if clamp and M.EXT:
            ext = push(d1, lambda s: M.proj(s, M.EXT))
            if len(ext) != 1: return None
            ref = next(iter(ext))
            d2 = push(d2, lambda st: tuple(ref[M.EXT.index(i)] if i in M.EXT else v for i, v in enumerate(st)))
    return d1, d2
def _block(M, reading): return (M.IN, 0) if reading == 'rest' else (M.MED, 1)
def diff_block(M, d1, d2, block):
    return tv(push(d1, lambda s: M.proj(s, block)), push(d2, lambda s: M.proj(s, block)))
def pairs_k_positions(alpha, L, k):
    for h in itertools.product(alpha, repeat=L):
        for pos in itertools.combinations(range(L), k):
            for alt in itertools.product(alpha, repeat=k):
                h2 = list(h)
                for p, a in zip(pos, alt): h2[p] = a
                if tuple(h2) != h: yield list(h), h2
def mon_accepts(mon, h):
    """mon = (m0, delta, accept): delta[(m, u)] -> m' (a missing entry means rejection)."""
    if mon is None: return True
    m = mon[0]
    for u in h:
        if (m, u) not in mon[1]: return False
        m = mon[1][(m, u)]
    return m in mon[2]
def witnesses(M, q, r, k, H, reading, init, clamp=True, mon=None):
    """Yield Tr witness pairs one by one; on a history pair for which clamping cannot be realized, set the obs flag"""
    block, cutf = _block(M, reading)
    st = {'obs': False}
    def gen():
        rest = ['rest'] * q
        for L in range(1, H + 1):
            for kk in range(1, min(k, L) + 1):
                for h1, h2 in pairs_k_positions(M.alpha, L, kk):
                    if not (mon_accepts(mon, h1) and mon_accepts(mon, h2)): continue
                    res = run_pair(M, h1 + rest, h2 + rest, init, clamp, ncut=q * cutf)
                    if res is None: st['obs'] = True; continue
                    if diff_block(M, *res, block) > r: yield h1, h2
    return gen(), st
@_guard(2)
def det_tr_enum(M, q=6, r=0.05, k=1, H=4, reading='rest', init=None, clamp=True, mon=None):
    e = validate_layer1_decl(M, q=q, k=k, H=H, r=r, mon=mon, reading=reading, init=init)
    if e: return e, None
    r = _q(r)
    if reading == 'cut' and M.cut is None: return UNDEF, None
    if not _block(M, reading)[0]: return 0, None
    g, st = witnesses(M, q, r, k, H, reading, init, clamp, mon)
    for w in g: return 1, w
    return (OBS if st['obs'] else 0), None
@_guard(6)
def det_tr_exact(M, q=6, k=1, reading='rest', init=None, r=0.05, mon=None):
    """Pair-automaton adjudication for finite models. Each transition is checked to be a point distribution, otherwise ⊥_Obs. When a monitor automaton is declared, the nodes are (s, s′, j, m, m′),
       and acceptance additionally requires m and m′ to be accepting states (Theorem 1 (c)). Returns (verdict, shortest history length, N, |I'|, search-derived bound, number of expanded nodes searched):
       N and I′ both come from this exhaustive search, so N + N·|I'|·k (multiplied by M_c² when there is a monitor) is a search-derived upper bound on history length, not an a priori bound for choosing H_max;
       an a priori bound must come from declared upper bounds on N and on the in-boundary state space |S_in| (Theorem 1 (a2)); this function does not provide one. The number of expanded nodes searched is an after-the-fact count."""
    e = validate_layer1_decl(M, q=q, k=k, r=r, mon=mon, reading=reading, init=init)
    if e: return e, None, None, None, None, None
    def nxt(st, u):
        d = M.cut(st) if u is CUT else M.step(st, u)
        if len(d) != 1: raise ValueError
~~~
